It verifies $480$ sector identities, all upper-incidence bounds, all pair-annihilator lower bounds, all auxiliary quartic Newton inequalities, and all $15/8$ middle bounds. Its computations use exact integer arithmetic; finite-field assignments are compared with the graph endpoint counts before they are accepted. These regressions supplement the universal proof, rather than replacing it. The proof of the upper incidence bound is the complete coefficientwise case analysis in Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:r7:lem:bounds}.

The previous structural report and its source archive are included unchanged. Its search discussion remains a historical record: the present theorem resolves the middle-gap question it left open. No numerical optimization or unsuccessful search is used in this proof. These are ordinary mathematical proofs with exact checks, not formal proof-assistant certification or external refereeing; no exhaustive priority claim is asserted.

%% ======================== source 03 ========================
\section{Eventual scaling for five-plus-three covers, one weighted shore (source~03)}\label{mrn:src:03}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~03, as delivered.} We prove eventual strict actual-rank ultra-log-concavity under common right-cover scaling for every bipartite graph with a minimum cover consisting of five left and three right vertices. Left activities are one; all fixed right activities are arbitrary and positive. For the conditional quintic obtained by forcing the three right-cover vertices, the new lower-middle factor is $77/48$, strictly exceeding the required $8/5$. The other two difficult factors are $7/4$, with strict inequality, and $55/24>16/7$. The proof combines a graph-generic additive plane-incidence correction, a small represented-matroid counting lemma, and exact comparisons with a Lorentzian auxiliary quintic. Together with earlier results, this leaves $4+4$ as the only unresolved minimum-cover split at rank eight. No general finite-activity theorem or global rank-eight eventual theorem is asserted.
\mnote{The $4+4$ split left open here is settled by source~02 (top gap) and source~01 (middle gap).}

\subsection{Main result and exact sectors}
For a finite bipartite graph $G=(X,Y)$, define its endpoint support polynomial by
\[
 P_G(t)=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq X,\ J\subseteq Y\\|I|=|J|,\ G[I,J]\text{ perfectly matchable}}}u_Iv_Jt^{|I|}.
\]
Each feasible endpoint pair is counted once. If the matching rank is $d$ and $P_G=\sum p_it^i$, its actual-rank Newton gaps are
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:gaps}
 G_i=i(d-i)p_i^2-(i+1)(d-i+1)p_{i-1}p_{i+1}.
\end{equation}
Nonnegative gaps and no internal zeros define $\ULC_d$.

Fix a minimum cover $A\sqcup B$, put $L=X\setminus A$, $R=Y\setminus B$, and assume $|A|=5$, $|B|=3$. Thus $d=8$, the exterior $L$--$B$ matching rank is three, and the $A$--$R$ rank is five. All left activities are one. Fix arbitrary positive right activities and multiply those on $B$ by a common parameter $\lambda>0$.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:e:fp:thm:main}
For every such graph and field, all actual-rank-eight gaps are strictly positive for sufficiently large $\lambda$. The threshold may depend on the graph, cover and fixed field.
\end{theorem}
Forcing all three $B$ vertices and dividing out their activities gives $t^3 C(t)$, where $C(t)=\sum_{j=0}^5 C_jt^j$ and every $C_j>0$. We prove
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:targets}
 C_1^2>2C_0C_2,\qquad
 C_2^2\ge\frac{77}{48}C_1C_3,\qquad
 C_3^2>\frac74C_2C_4,\qquad
 C_4^2\ge\frac{55}{24}C_3C_5.
\end{equation}
The actual-rank-eight factors at these four indices are $25/16$, $8/5$, $7/4$, and $16/7$. The two numerical margins are
\[
 \frac{77}{48}-\frac85=\frac1{240},\qquad
 \frac{55}{24}-\frac{16}{7}=\frac1{168}.
\]

Use independent indeterminates for the graph edges. A square minor is nonzero exactly when the corresponding endpoints are perfectly matchable. Let $K$ be the $5\times3$ core matrix and $q\ge1$ the number of independent triples of the exterior rows $\ell\in L$. Remove isolated rows. For each independent labeled pair $P$ of $L$, choose its annihilator $v_P$ and introduce the virtual point $Kv_P$. Keep every label, including loops and parallel images. Adjoin the five private coordinate vectors $E$ in the $A$ space.

The resulting rank-five matroid has a Lorentzian basis polynomial \cite{b:BH}. Mark private elements by $s$, original $R$ elements by $t$ times their activities, and virtual elements by $z$, of activity one. Call the result $F(s,t,z)$. Its coefficients are ordinary basis counts without factorial factors. Let $T_j,P_j,Q_j,R_j$ count bases with $j$ original $R$ elements and respectively zero, one, two, three virtual elements; the remaining elements are private. There are no bases with four virtual elements. Let $N_j,Z_j$ be the forced endpoint sectors with respectively one and zero selected original $L$ rows. Exact elimination and private complementation give
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:sectors}
 C_j=qT_j+P_j+N_j+Z_j,
\end{equation}
with impossible terms zero. Three original $L$ rows give the first term, two give the virtual term, and the other sectors remain explicitly indicated.

Put $f(t,u)=F(qu,t,u)$ and $b_j=[t^ju^{5-j}]f$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:bj}
 \begin{aligned}
 b_1&=q^4T_1+q^3P_1+q^2Q_1+qR_1,\\
 b_2&=q^3T_2+q^2P_2+qQ_2+R_2,\\
 b_3&=q^2T_3+qP_3+Q_3,\qquad
 b_4=C_4,\qquad b_5=C_5/q.
 \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
Every quotient used below is by fixed core, private and original $R$ data, independent of the original $L$ edge indeterminates.

\subsection{A graph generic additive plane bound}
Let $\mathcal L$ be a loopless rank-three row configuration and $H$ a plane. Let $q$ count its bases, $\beta$ its elements outside $H$, and $D_H$ unordered pairs of pair-annihilators independent after restriction to $H$. The earlier rank-seven report \mnote{source~08, Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:r7:lem:bounds} and Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:r7:lem:adjoint}} \cite{b:m08} proves, even with arbitrary positive original weights,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:old}
 \frac34q\beta\le D_H\le q\beta.
\end{equation}
The upper inequality is coefficientwise. Its proof classifies restrictions to at most four original labels. The lower inequality is a rank-three Lorentzian Hessian inequality. Both proofs are included in the unchanged companion.

At unit weights, if $F_{\mathcal L}$ counts independent triples of labeled pair-annihilators, applying \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:old} to $H=\spn(P)$ and summing the marked pair gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:adjoint}
 \frac34q^2\le F_{\mathcal L}\le q^2.
\end{equation}
Indeed $3F_{\mathcal L}=\sum_P D_P$ and $\sum_P\beta_P=3q$. No independence of $H$ from $\mathcal L$ is needed in \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:old} or \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:adjoint}.

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:fp:lem:additive}
Suppose each permitted coordinate of each row of $\mathcal L$ is a separate algebraically independent indeterminate over a characteristic-zero field, and $H$ is defined over that field. With unit original activities,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:additive}
 4D_H\ge q(3\beta+1).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Rescale nonzero coordinates of a normal to $H$ to one. Up to coordinate permutation its support mask is $1$, $3$, or $7$. For two row masks $S,T$, the cross-product support consists of the missing coordinate for every feasible matching of the two rows to two distinct coordinates. For four distinct row labels, the determinant of their two cross products and the fixed normal is nonzero exactly when these three supports match all three coordinates. A monomial chooses one coordinate from each original row; this uniquely fixes the two cross-product coordinates and the normal coordinate. Distinct normal terms therefore cannot cancel. For a shared row use
\[
 \det(a\mathbin\times b,a\mathbin\times c,n_H)
   =\pm\det(a,b,c)(a\cdot n_H).
\]

Expand $4D_H-3q\beta-q$ in products of binomial population coefficients. On a three-row mask multiset admitting a basis, its coefficient is the number of outside rows minus one. On a four-row multiset, it is $4E-3Q$, where $E$ counts good pair partitions and $Q$ counts outside-label deletions leaving a basis. There are $84$ triple and $210$ quadruple profiles for each normal support. The complete exact matching enumeration gives respectively $138,153,144$ nonzero coefficients for supports $1,3,7$. The only negative coefficients are four occurrences of $-4$, for support $3$, on
\[
 (1,2,5,6),\quad(1,5,6,6),\quad(2,5,5,6),\quad(5,5,6,6).
\]
The bundled standard-library checker \mnote{shipped as \path{code/03-five-plus-three-*.py}, outputs \path{data/03-five-plus-three-*.json}} reconstructs every profile. All remaining binomial-population terms are nonnegative, so it suffices to handle these four row types.

Write their populations as $A,B,x,y$, respectively, and put
\[
 M=A+x,\quad N=B+y,\quad
 f=Ax+\binom{x}{2},\quad g=By+\binom{y}{2},\quad
 \alpha=A(A-1),\quad\gamma=B(B-1).
\]
Within the two mixed coordinate planes, independent pairs share one intersection line in $H$, of combined mass $f+g$. Cross pairs have direction-class masses $AB$, then $B$ repeated $x$ times, $A$ repeated $y$ times, and $1$ repeated $xy$ times. Thus
\[
 q=fN+Mg,\qquad\beta=M+N,\qquad
 D_H=(f+g)MN+\frac{(MN)^2-xy-xB^2-yA^2-A^2B^2}{2}.
\]
If $M N=0$, the result is immediate. Otherwise direct expansion, with $F_0=4D_H-q(3\beta+1)$, gives
\begin{align*}
 2F_0={}&MN\bigl((M-N)^2+M+N-2\bigr)\\
 &+\alpha N(3N-M-3)+\gamma M(3M-N-3)-4\alpha\gamma.
\end{align*}
For fixed positive integers $M,N$, this is bilinear on
$0\le\alpha\le M(M-1)$, $0\le\gamma\le N(N-1)$. Its four corner values are
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 MN\bigl((M-N)^2+M+N-2\bigr),\qquad
 MN(N-1)(M+N-1),\\
 MN(M-1)(M+N-1),\qquad0.
 \end{gathered}
\]
All are nonnegative. This proves the remaining subsystem and hence the lemma.
\end{proof}
The independence condition is essential: the affine hexagon $(2,0)$, $(1,1)$, $(-1,1)$, $(-2,0)$, $(-1,-1)$, $(1,-1)$, lifted to $(x,y,1)$, with $H=\{z=0\}$, has $q=20$, $\beta=6$, $D_H=93<95$. We never use \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:additive} for a plane spanned by original $L$ rows.

\subsection{The top conditional gap}
Fix an independent triple $J\subseteq R$ and quotient by its span. If the quotient core has rank below two, both its $N_3$ and $Q_3$ contributions vanish. Otherwise its row plane $H$ is independent of all $L$ entries, and \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:additive} applies. Write $C_J=qU_J+P_J+\beta_J$ for this sector of $C_3$.

If $n$ is the number of nonloop original rows, elementary basis extension gives $q\ge n-2$. Let $h$ count independent original pairs not spanning $H$. Then $h\ge n-1$: if $H$ contains $g\ge1$ original rows, the $g\beta$ cross pairs give $g\beta\ge n-1$; if it contains none, basis extension gives at least $2n-3$ independent pairs. Private coordinates span the quotient, so $U_J\ge1$ and $P_J\ge h$. Therefore
\[
 C_J\ge2n-3+\beta_J,\qquad
 q\beta_J-D_J\le\frac q4(\beta_J-1)\le\frac q{12}C_J,
\]
since $\beta_J\le n$. Deficient quotient maps satisfy the final inequality with zero loss, without any additive correction. Summing with positive activities gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:b3}
 \frac{11}{12}qC_3\le b_3\le qC_3.
\end{equation}
The upper bound follows from \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:old}. Degree-five Newton at index four now yields
\[
 C_4^2=b_4^2\ge\frac52 b_3b_5\ge\frac{55}{24}C_3C_5.
\]

\subsection{The strict next to top gap}
Fix an independent pair $J\subseteq R$ and quotient by it, leaving dimension three. Let $E$ be the nonloop private images, $m=|E|\ge3$, and $V$ their basis count. Let $X,N,Z$ be the fixed-$J$ sectors with two, one, zero original $L$ rows, respectively. Thus this contribution to $C_2$ is $qV+X+N+Z$. Let $W$ count private images whose further quotient core has rank two. The additive plane bound and the old adjoint bound give, after summation,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:b2lower}
 b_2\ge q^2\left(C_2-\frac{N_2+Z_2-W_2}{4}\right).
\end{equation}
Here $W_2$ is the activity-weighted sum of these fixed-$J$ counts.

We prove, for each fixed $J$,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:3unit}
 N+Z-2W<qV+X.
\end{equation}
We have $q\ge n-2$, $V\ge m-2$, and
\[
 2X\ge W(n-1),
\]
because each qualifying private image has at least $n-1$ virtual completions, and each resulting private pair is counted twice.

If the quotient core has rank at most one, $N=Z=W=0$ and $qV>0$. If it has rank two, let its image plane be $P$. Exactly the private images outside $P$ qualify. Thus $W\ge1$, $N=W\beta\le Wn$, $Z=0$, and $V\ge\max(1,W-2)$. For $W=1,2$, the bound $qV+X\ge n-2+W(n-1)/2$ is strictly greater than $W(n-2)$. For $W\ge4$, its margin is at least
\[
 (W/2-2)(n-1)+2>0.
\]
For $W=3,V\ge2$, the margin is at least $(n+1)/2$. If $W=3,V=1$, the private images are exactly three basis vectors outside $P$. Every nonzero virtual image in $P$ has at least two nonzero coordinates in that basis, and hence at least two private-pair completions. Thus $X\ge2(n-1)$ and $qV+X\ge3n-4>3n-6\ge N-2W$.

If the quotient core has rank three, $W=m$, $Z=1$, $N\le mn$. For $m\ge4$ the margin in \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:3unit} is at least
\[
 (m/2-2)(n-1)+1>0.
\]
For $m=3$, the private images form a basis with matrix $E$, and $V=1$. The transformed row matrix is $M=L K_J^{-1}E$. Its nonzero entries, two-minors and three-minors count $N,X,q$, respectively. It has rank three and no zero row. Write $p$ for its independent-pair count; basis extension gives $p\ge2n-3$. If $N\le3n-1$, then $q+X\ge q+p\ge3n-5\ge N-4$. If $N=3n$, every row has full support, so the cross product of any independent pair has at least two nonzero coordinates. Hence $q+X\ge q+2p\ge5n-8\ge N-4$. In both cases $q+X>N-5=N+Z-2W$. This proves \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:3unit}.

Summing gives $N_2+Z_2-W_2<C_2/2$, so \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:b2lower} yields
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:b2bounds}
 \frac78q^2C_2<b_2\le q^2C_2.
\end{equation}
For the upper bound use $Q_2\le qN_2$ and $R_2=F_{\mathcal L}Z_2\le q^2Z_2$. Newton at index three, together with \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:b3}, now gives
\[
 q^2C_3^2\ge b_3^2\ge2b_2b_4>\frac74q^2C_2C_4.
\]

\subsection{A four dimensional unit counting lemma}
The remaining comparison needs a quotient by just one $R$ vector. Its private configuration spans dimension four and has at most five elements. We prove the required count independently of graph genericity.

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:fp:lem:2point}
Let $E$ span dimension four and have at most five unit-weighted labeled elements. For independent $v_1,v_2$, let $V$ count $E$ bases, $X_i$ count $E$ triples completing $v_i$, and $N_{12}$ count $E$ pairs completing both. Then $V+X_1+X_2\ge N_{12}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Append a loop if $E$ has four elements. Dualize the rank-four matroid on these five labels and the two marked labels. Its dual has rank three; both its marked pair and its $E$ restriction have full respective ranks two and three. Basis complementation transforms the assertion into
\[
 q(E)\le P(v_1)+P(v_2)+\beta,
\]
where $H=\spn(v_1,v_2)$, $\beta$ counts $E$ outside $H$, and $P(v_i)$ counts pairs completing $v_i$.

Let $f$ count nonloop $E$ elements in $H$, and let $p,Q$ count independent pairs and triples outside $H$. Then $\beta+f\le5$, $\beta\ge1$, and
\[
 P(v_1)+P(v_2)\ge\beta f+p,\qquad
 q(E)\le\beta\binom f2+pf+Q.
\]
For $\beta\ge2$, use $p\le\binom\beta2$, $Q\le\binom\beta3$, and $3Q\le(\beta-2)p$. The upper bounds for $q-P(v_1)-P(v_2)-\beta$, at $f=0,1,2,3$, are respectively
\[
 -\beta,\qquad-2\beta+Q,\qquad-2\beta+p+Q,\qquad-\beta+2p+Q.
\]
The last three have respectively $\beta\le4,3,2$, and are all nonpositive.

For $\beta=1$, $q(E)$ is the independent-pair count of the $f\le4$ inside elements. If $c_i$ count those parallel to $v_i$, then $P(v_1)+P(v_2)=2f-c_1-c_2$. For $f\le3$, this is at least $f\ge\binom f2$. For $f=4$, either $c_1+c_2\le3$, giving $P(v_1)+P(v_2)+1\ge6$, or all four lie in the two marked directions, giving $q=c_1c_2\le4<5$. This proves the lemma.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:fp:lem:baseline}
Let $E$ be as above and let $v_1,v_2,v_3$ be arbitrary. Let $X$ count one marked element and three private elements forming a basis, $N$ count two marked and two private elements, and $Z$ count three marked and one private element. Let $W$ count independent private pairs $P$ for which $\rk(P+\spn(v_1,v_2,v_3))=4$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:baseline}
 V+X\ge N+Z-2W.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Quotient by an independent private pair $P$. Remaining private images span dimension two. Let $n_P$ count independent pairs of marked images, and $m_P$ mixed bases with one marked and one private image. If the marked rank is two, $n_P=1,2,3$ gives respectively $m_P\ge2,3,4$: retain two independent private images, and in the last case note that the three marked directions are distinct. Hence $m_P-3n_P+6\ge1$. At smaller marked rank, $n_P=0$ and $m_P\ge0$. Summing over $P$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:mixed}
 3(X-N+2W)\ge W.
\end{equation}
If the original marked rank is at most two, $Z=0$ and the result follows.

Otherwise let $z=Z$ count private elements outside the marked plane. If $E$ has five nonloop elements, its unique circuit has size $c\ge2$ and $V=c$. Every pair is independent except possibly a parallel pair when $c=2$. Thus
\[
 W=10-\binom{5-z}{2}-\epsilon,
\]
where $\epsilon\in\{0,1\}$ is one only for a parallel pair outside the marked plane. For $z\le2$, $z-V\le0$; for $z=3,4,5$, respectively, $W\ge8,9,9$ whereas $3(z-V)\le3,6,9$. Therefore $W\ge3(z-V)$ and \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:mixed} proves the assertion.

Otherwise, after removing loops, $E$ is a four-element basis. Here $V=1$ and $W=6-\binom{4-z}{2}$. Equation \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:mixed} suffices for $z\le3$. For $z=4$, write the marked vectors as columns of a $4\times3$ matrix $K$. Every three-row minor is nonzero; $X,N$ count its nonzero entries and two-minors. We need $N-X\le9$. If $X\ge9$, use $N\le18$. If $X\le8$ and a row has singleton support, its three row pairs contribute at most $X-1$ two-minors and the other three at most nine, giving $N\le X+8$. Otherwise all four rows have support two. Two share a support; their independent pair gives exactly one nonzero two-minor, and the other five give at most fifteen. Thus $N\le16<X+9$. This proves \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:baseline}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:fp:lem:4unit}
Fix a core map from dimension three to dimension four and private $E$ as in Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:fp:lem:2point}. For any loopless unit-weighted original $L$ configuration of rank three, let $q,V,X,N,Z,W$ denote the corresponding basis, one-virtual, one-original-row, no-original-row and qualifying-private-pair counts. Then
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:4unit}
 qV+X\ge N+Z-2W.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Start with three original rows forming a basis. Their pair annihilators form the dual basis, and their images are the three marked vectors of Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:fp:lem:baseline}. For each private pair, its one-original-row count equals the number of independent pairs of these marked images. Thus that lemma proves the starting inequality.

Add a nonzero original row $\ell$. The increment of $q$ counts old pairs complementary to $\ell$ and is at least one. New virtual points are the images of $\ell\mathbin\times\ell_i$. If their image has rank below two, the increment of $N$ is zero, so the slack cannot decrease. Otherwise choose two independent new virtual points $v_1,v_2$. They span that image plane. The increment of $N$ is exactly $N_{12}$, the private-pair count complementary to it; the increment of $X$ is at least $X_1+X_2$. Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:fp:lem:2point} shows that $qV+X-N$ cannot decrease. Finally $Z,W$ depend only on the core and private configuration, and are unchanged. Induction proves \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:4unit}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The lower middle gap and eventual scaling}
Fix one original $R$ vector and quotient by it. The five private images satisfy Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:fp:lem:4unit}. The fixed-vector sector of $C_1$ is $qV+X+N+Z$. Each qualifying private pair has a rank-two quotient core independent of the original $L$ entries. Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:fp:lem:additive} therefore bounds its two-virtual count below by $q(3\beta+1)/4$. The three-virtual count is $F_{\mathcal L}Z\ge3q^2Z/4$. No four virtual elements can form a basis. Summing with the positive original $R$ activities gives
\[
 b_1\ge q^3\left(C_1-\frac{N_1+Z_1-W_1}{4}\right).
\]
Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:fp:lem:4unit} gives $N_1+Z_1-W_1\le C_1/2$, and hence
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:fp:eq:b1}
 b_1\ge\frac78q^3C_1.
\end{equation}
Newton at index two has factor two. By \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:b2bounds}, \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:b3}, and \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:b1},
\[
 q^4C_2^2\ge b_2^2\ge2b_1b_3
 \ge2\cdot\frac78\cdot\frac{11}{12}q^4C_1C_3
 =\frac{77}{48}q^4C_1C_3.
\]
Cancel $q^4>0$ to obtain the second inequality in \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:targets}.

The remaining first inequality follows from the ambient transversal matroid after contracting $B$. Its degree is $n+2$, so its first Newton factor is $2(n+2)/(n+1)>2$. This proves all of \eqref{mrn:e:fp:eq:targets}. At indices below three, the leading scaling polynomial is the pure right-shore-$B$ endpoint polynomial, which is $\ULC_3$ by its rank-three basis representation. Its normalization strictly improves the actual-rank-eight one. At index three, the square term has a higher power of $\lambda$ than the neighboring product. At higher indices the leading gaps are precisely the four strict conditional gaps just proved. Every gap polynomial therefore has a positive leading coefficient and is positive for sufficiently large $\lambda$. This proves Theorem~\ref{mrn:e:fp:thm:main}.

For a gap $G(\lambda)=a_D\lambda^D+\sum_{i<D}a_i\lambda^i$ with $a_D>0$, the explicit field-dependent choice $\lambda\ge1+\sum_{i<D}|a_i|/a_D$ makes the leading term dominate the others. Taking the maximum gives a sufficient threshold. No uniform graph-independent threshold is asserted.

\subsection{Scope and exact verification}
The earlier general top-gap theorem, included in the structural companion inside \cite{b:m08}, already proves eventual strict scaling for $3+5$ covers. \mnote{Source~08s, Corollary~\ref{mrn:e:tg:cor:cubic}.} Covers with one shore of size at most two satisfy actual-rank ULC at every positive field by the earlier small-cover theorem, whose full proof is also bundled there. Hence $4+4$ is the only unresolved rank-eight cover split. The known persistent-failure bracket remains $[8,38]$; this report narrows the possible rank-eight case without proving that case. General finite-activity rank-six and higher one-shore ULC remains separate.

The additive plane proof uses the complete finite set of $882$ mask profiles, followed by the analytic four-variable argument for all populations. It is not a bounded population-grid argument. The portable checker also verifies the four-variable identity and all four corner formulas by exact polynomial arithmetic. A separate exact endpoint/finite-field regression checks $64$ genuine $5+3$ configurations, $384$ endpoint-sector identities, $384$ fixed-$R$ unit inequalities, and all three new gap comparisons. Each represented endpoint coefficient is checked against direct Boolean matching before use. A $13{,}056$-case binary configuration diagnostic checks the small four-dimensional baseline independently; no part of the proof relies on this diagnostic covering characteristic zero.

The rank-seven PDF and source archive are included unchanged, providing the earlier incidence, ambient-normalization and small-cover inputs. These are ordinary mathematical proofs with exact checks, not proof-assistant certification or external refereeing. No exhaustive priority claim is made.

%% ======================== source 02 ========================
\section{The top conditional gap for four-plus-four covers, one weighted shore (source~02)}\label{mrn:src:02}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~02, as delivered.} For every genuine $4+4$ minimum cover, unit left activities and arbitrary positive right activities, we prove a strict top conditional Newton gap with the actual-rank-eight factor $16/7$. The proof uses two finite reductions valid for arbitrarily many exterior vertices: a coefficientwise upper plane-incidence bound for graph-generic rank-four configurations, and a coupled unit-count inequality whose four-label contribution supplies a strict positive correction. All finite profiles are checked by exact standard-library programs and an independently implemented replay. An explicit six-vector real example shows that the unrestricted upper-incidence bound is false, so the graph-generic scope matters. Together with the preceding $5+3$ theorem, only the $4+4$ middle conditional comparison remains in the rank-eight eventual-scaling problem. We do not claim a complete rank-eight theorem.
\mnote{The remaining $4+4$ middle comparison is proved by source~01, which completes the rank-eight theorem.}

\subsection{Graph normalization and the new top gap}
Let $G=(X,Y)$ be a finite bipartite graph and let
\[
 P_G(t)=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq X,\ J\subseteq Y\\|I|=|J|,\ G[I,J]\text{ perfectly matchable}}}u_Iv_Jt^{|I|}.
\]
Every feasible endpoint pair is counted once. For matching rank $d$ and $P_G=\sum p_it^i$, actual-rank ultra-log-concavity requires
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:gap}
 i(d-i)p_i^2\ge(i+1)(d-i+1)p_{i-1}p_{i+1}
\end{equation}
and no internal zeros.

Fix a minimum cover $A\sqcup B$ with $|A|=|B|=4$. Put $L=X\setminus A$, $R=Y\setminus B$. The graph has matching rank eight, and both exterior matching ranks are four. All left activities are one, and right activities are arbitrary fixed positive numbers. Forcing every $B$ vertex into the selected right endpoint set and dividing out its activity product gives
\[
 t^4 C(t),\qquad C(t)=\sum_{j=0}^4C_jt^j,\qquad C_j>0.
\]
Let $S_R$ be the activity-weighted number of independent exterior-right pairs in the generic $A$--$R$ representation. Exterior rank four gives $S_R>0$.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:e:ff:thm:top}
For every such graph and field,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:top}
 C_3^2\ge\frac{16}{7}C_2C_4+\frac8{21}S_RC_4
 >\frac{16}{7}C_2C_4.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
The factor $16/7$ is the top conditional factor required by \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:gap} at actual rank eight. The ambient matroid first gap already exceeds the required $8/5$. The remaining conditional target is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:open}
 C_2^2\ge\frac74C_1C_3,
\end{equation}
with strictness or a separate treatment of equality needed to finish eventual scaling. \mnote{Proved, with strictness, by source~01 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:01}).} The preceding companion \cite{b:m03} handles $5+3$ covers and, together with earlier results bundled there, every other rank-eight cover split except $4+4$.

\subsection{Incidence quantities and a real obstruction}
Let $\mathcal L$ be a rank-four row configuration and $H$ a two-plane in its ambient space. Let $q$ count weighted independent four-subsets and $\beta$ weighted original pairs complementary to $H$. For each independent labeled triple $P$, choose an annihilator $v_P$ with activity equal to the product of the original activities. Let $D_H$ count unordered pairs of these virtual labels whose restrictions to $H$ are independent. Thus $q\beta$ and $D_H$ are homogeneous of degree six in the original activities. At unit activities also let $h_H$ count original independent triples whose hyperplane does not contain $H$.

The universal rank-four lower incidence inequality is
\[
 D_H\ge\frac23q\beta,
\]
from the diffuse-plane Lorentzian Hessian construction in the earlier structural work included with \cite{b:m03}. \mnote{Source~08s's Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:tg:lem:plane} at $b=4$.} The reverse companion $D_H\le q\beta$ is not valid for arbitrary represented configurations.

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:ff:lem:counter}
With unit activities, take the six real vectors
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 (1,0,-1,0),\quad(0,1,3,0),\quad(1,0,0,-1),\\
 (1,1,0,2),\quad(0,1,0,0),\quad(1,1,0,0),
 \end{gathered}
\]
and $H=\spn(e_3,e_4)$. Then $q=12$, $\beta=12$, and $D_H=145>144=q\beta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The only dependent four-subsets are $1234$, $1256$, and $3456$. The only original pairs not complementary to $H$ are $13$, $25$, and $46$. Every original triple is independent and has a nonzero annihilator restriction. The five projective restriction classes, with directions
\[
 (0,1),\ (1,0),\ (1,1),\ (1,3),\ (2,-1),
\]
have masses $6,6,6,1,1$. Therefore
\[
 D_H=\frac{20^2-3\cdot6^2-1^2-1^2}{2}=145.
\]
The bundled integer-determinant checker lists every triple in its class. It also verifies the squarefree six-label coefficient: $q\beta$ contributes nine and $D_H$ ten.
\end{proof}
The displayed vectors satisfy special algebraic dependencies. This example is not a graph-generic counterexample under the hypotheses below.

\subsection{A coefficientwise graph generic upper bound}
We say that $\mathcal L$ is graph-generic over a characteristic-zero field $F$ when each allowed row coordinate is a separate algebraically independent indeterminate over $F$. Every forbidden coordinate is zero. The fixed plane $H$ is defined over $F$, independently of all these indeterminates.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:e:ff:thm:upper}
For graph-generic $\mathcal L$ and such an independent two-plane $H$,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:upper}
 D_H\le q\beta
\end{equation}
coefficientwise in the original activities. In particular it holds for arbitrary positive activities and arbitrary numbers of original rows.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
First consider a degree-six monomial using repeated labels. A term of $D_H$ is formed from two distinct independent triples; their intersection $S$ has size one or two. If $S$ is dependent, both coefficients vanish. If $\spn(S)$ meets $H$ nontrivially, both annihilator restrictions vanish on that intersection and cannot be independent. No pair containing $S$ can complement $H$, so the $q\beta$ coefficient also vanishes.

Otherwise contract $\spn(S)$. The image of $H$ still has dimension two, and the two coefficients become the corresponding squarefree coefficients for rank $4-|S|$. For one shared label the coefficientwise rank-three upper-incidence theorem in the earlier rank-seven report applies. For two shared labels this is the rank-two identity: two point annihilators restrict independently to the whole quotient exactly when the two points form a basis. The cited rank-three bound is universal for represented configurations, so the contraction does not need to preserve graph genericity.

It remains to consider six distinct labels. Their supports are nonempty masks in $\{1,\ldots,15\}$. Up to relabeling, there are $\binom{20}{6}=38{,}760$ nondecreasing support multisets. Represent $H$ by a $4\times2$ matrix. Its four rows form a rank-two configuration; up to coordinate permutation its loop and parallel-class patterns have exactly seven types:
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:types}
 \begin{gathered}
 (0,0,1,2),\ (0,1,1,2),\ (0,1,2,3),\ (1,1,1,2),\\
 (1,1,2,2),\ (1,1,2,3),\ (1,2,3,4).
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Here zero denotes a loop, and equal positive entries denote parallel rows. Let $\mathcal B_H$ be its set of independent coordinate pairs.

An original pair complements $H$ exactly when it can match two coordinates whose complement belongs to $\mathcal B_H$. A triple annihilator can have a nonzero coordinate $i$ exactly when its three rows can match the other three coordinates. Two disjoint triple annihilators restrict independently exactly when two such possible missing coordinates form an element of $\mathcal B_H$. These are exact polynomial nonvanishing tests: each monomial chooses one coordinate from every independently parametrized original row and uniquely determines the two missing coordinates. Different minors of $H$ cannot cancel.

For each support profile compute
\begin{align*}
 Q&=\sum_{\substack{P\subseteq[6]\\|P|=2}}
 [P\text{ complements }H]\,[[6]\setminus P\text{ is a basis}],\\
 D_6&=\sum_{\{P,P^c\}:|P|=3}
 [\text{the two annihilator restrictions are independent}].
\end{align*}
These are exactly the coefficients of $q\beta$ and $D_H$. The complete exact table verifies $Q-D_6\ge0$ in all $271{,}320$ cases. Its zero-coefficient counts, in the order \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:types}, are
\[
 32{,}570,\ 25{,}036,\ 20{,}670,\ 23{,}574,\
 18{,}932,\ 17{,}732,\ 16{,}532.
\]
Every other coefficient is positive. Both the producer and a separately implemented coordinate-injection replay, with identical per-type hashes, are included. This finite classification exhausts every remaining monomial and proves \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:upper}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A coupled unit correction}
Separate worst-case bounds for $D_H$, $q$, and $h_H$ lose too much at rank eight. The following coupled inequality supplies the required correction.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:e:ff:thm:coupled}
Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{mrn:e:ff:thm:upper}, now with every original activity one,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:coupled}
 T:=7D_H+q^2+qh_H-6q\beta\ge q>0.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Group every product by the union of its original labels. Contributions using seven or eight labels come only from $q^2$ and $qh_H$, and are nonnegative. No contribution uses fewer than four labels.

On four labels, a nonbasis contributes zero. A basis contributes one to $q^2$, $h_B$ to $qh_H$, and $\beta_B$ to $q\beta$. Two triples with this union share a pair; their restrictions are independent exactly when that pair complements $H$. Hence $D_H$ contributes $\beta_B$, and the total is
\[
 1+h_B+\beta_B\ge1.
\]
Summing over bases already gives the lower bound $q$ in \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:coupled}. It remains to check unions of five and six labels.

For a fixed $k$-label profile, $k=5,6$, put
\begin{align*}
 A_k&=\#\{\text{ordered pairs of bases with union }[k]\},\\
 B_k&=\#\{\text{basis and good triple with union }[k]\},\\
 Q_k&=\#\{\text{basis and complementary pair with union }[k]\},\\
 D_k&=\#\{\text{unordered triple pairs with union }[k]
       \text{ and independent restrictions}\}.
\end{align*}
A good triple is independent and its hyperplane does not contain $H$. The coefficient to check is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:coefficient}
 7D_k+A_k+B_k-6Q_k.
\end{equation}
For six labels, every quantity is an exact support-matching count as in the preceding proof. All $271{,}320$ coefficients are nonnegative.

For five labels, $A_5,B_5,Q_5$ are exact matching counts, while we certify a lower bound on $D_5$ by integer determinant witnesses. The two triples have exactly one shared row. The next elementary observation ensures that these witnesses are uniform over every plane of the same type, not just the numerical representative chosen for the calculation.

Write the triple annihilators as $v,w$. Their restriction determinant is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:minorpoly}
 \sum_{i<j}(v_iw_j-v_jw_i)\,\Delta_{ij}(H).
\end{equation}
A cofactor coordinate $v_i$ uses every ambient coordinate once except $i$. Thus every monomial in $v_iw_j$ has total coordinate multidegree
\[
 (2,2,2,2)-e_i-e_j.
\]
This identifies the unordered pair $\{i,j\}$ uniquely, even when row labels are shared. Each row-coordinate monomial in \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:minorpoly} therefore has coefficient equal to an integer multiple of a single $H$ minor. Terms using that minor may cancel, but terms using different minors cannot. Polynomial nonvanishing depends only on which $H$ minors vanish.

Represent a nonzero row of plane class $c$ by $(1,c)$, and a loop by $(0,0)$. For each nondecreasing five-row support profile, substitute the allowed entries from the fixed matrix
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:witnessmatrix}
 \begin{pmatrix}
 86&53&34&14\\96&50&25&15\\71&72&73&66\\96&20&54&39\\95&26&69&13
 \end{pmatrix},
\end{equation}
setting forbidden entries to zero. A nonzero evaluated determinant certifies a nonzero polynomial. By the single-minor property, that polynomial is nonzero for every $H$ of the same type. An accidentally zero specialization can only reduce the witnessed count $\underline D_5\le D_5$.

The exact certificate verifies
\[
 7\underline D_5+A_5+B_5-6Q_5\ge0
\]
in all $7\binom{19}{5}=81{,}396$ five-label cases. This proves \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:coefficient} on five labels as well. Combined with the four-label gain and the nonnegative larger unions, it establishes \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:coupled} for arbitrarily many original rows.
\end{proof}
The finite checks in this proof are complete coefficient reductions. They do not impose a population bound. The entries of \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:witnessmatrix} are nonvanishing witnesses after a uniformity proof, rather than random numerical evidence for the inequality.

\subsection{The top conditional comparison}
Represent graph edges by independent indeterminates, and let $K$ be the $4\times4$ core. Let $q\ge1$ count exterior $L$ bases. Introduce one virtual right point $Kv_P$ for each independent exterior $L$ triple $P$, retaining all labels. Adjoin the four private $A$ coordinates. The resulting rank-four matroid has a Lorentzian basis polynomial \cite{b:BH}. Mark its private, original $R$, and virtual elements by $s,t,z$, with the actual positive activities on $R$. Put $f(t,u)=F(qu,t,u)$ and $b_j=[t^ju^{4-j}]f$. Exact endpoint elimination gives
\[
 b_3=C_3,\qquad b_4=C_4/q,\qquad
 b_2=qC_2-\sum_Jv_J(q\beta_J-D_J),
\]
where $J$ runs over independent exterior $R$ pairs.

Quotient by such a fixed pair $J$. If the induced $2\times4$ core has rank two, its row space is a two-plane $H$ independent of all original $L$ entries. The corresponding endpoint sector is
\[
 C_J=qU_J+P_J+\beta_J.
\]
Private images span the quotient, so $U_J\ge1$ and every good virtual point has at least one private completion: $P_J\ge h_J$. Thus $C_J\ge q+h_J+\beta_J$. By Theorem~\ref{mrn:e:ff:thm:coupled},
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:ff:eq:loss}
 q\beta_J-D_J
 \le\frac q7(q+h_J+\beta_J-1)
 \le\frac q7(C_J-1).
\end{equation}
If the quotient core has rank below two, $\beta_J=D_J=0$ while $C_J\ge q\ge1$, so \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:loss} remains true without applying the incidence theorem. Summing yields
\[
 b_2\ge\frac67qC_2+\frac q7S_R.
\]
Degree-four Newton at index three has factor $8/3$. Consequently
\[
 C_3^2=b_3^2\ge\frac83b_2b_4
 \ge\frac{16}{7}C_2C_4+\frac8{21}S_RC_4,
\]
which proves Theorem~\ref{mrn:e:ff:thm:top}. The separate upper theorem also gives $b_2\le qC_2$, a potentially useful comparison for the remaining middle gap.

\subsection{Status and reproducibility}
The graph-generic upper proof uses $271{,}320$ complete six-label profiles, with repeated-label cases handled by contraction. The coupled proof uses another $271{,}320$ six-label profiles and $81{,}396$ five-label profiles. An independent implementation reconstructs all support predicates by explicit coordinate injections, rather than the producer's endpoint dynamic programming. For five-label witnesses it uses signed-permutation cofactors and the Cauchy--Binet expression \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:minorpoly}, rather than the producer's projected two-vectors. It independently evaluates $1{,}220{,}940$ witness determinants. Every coefficient histogram and all 21 profile-stream hashes agree.

The independent replay also expands the universal shared-row determinant symbolically: its $432$ nominal terms give $408$ distinct row-coordinate monomials, of which $24$ cancel and $384$ survive. Every survivor uses exactly one $H$ minor, agreeing with the multidegree proof. All checks use exact integer arithmetic and remain active under Python's optimization flag. The complete code, data, and ordinary source are included.

Under common scaling of $B$, the low-index gaps have positive leading terms by the pure-shore theorem, the first conditional gap is stronger than $8/5$, and Theorem~\ref{mrn:e:ff:thm:top} settles the top one strictly. For $4+4$, only \eqref{mrn:e:ff:eq:open} and its possible equality cases remain. A zero leading middle gap would still require examining lower-order scaling coefficients; searching only for a negative leading coefficient is a sufficient discovery route, not an exhaustive failure test.

The preceding $5+3$ PDF and source archive are included unchanged. They also contain the sealed rank-seven and earlier structural prerequisites. The persistent-failure bracket remains $[8,38]$, while the general finite-activity question is separate. \mnote{Improved to $[9,38]$ by source~01.} These are ordinary mathematical proofs supported by exact certificates, not formal proof-assistant certification or external refereeing; no exhaustive priority claim is asserted.

%% ======================== source 01 ========================
\section{Eventual one-shore scaling through rank eight (source~01)}\label{mrn:src:01}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~01, as delivered.} For every bipartite graph of matching rank at most eight, unit activities on the entire left shore, and any fixed positive right field, common scaling of the right part of a fixed minimum cover is eventually ultra-log-concave after normalization by the actual matching rank. The new case is the middle conditional gap of a $4+4$ cover. We obtain it from an auxiliary quartic using a rank-three minor-count potential, an exact parallel-class identity, and a complete finite hyperplane-incidence certificate. Ordering the exterior rows by their parallel classes supplies the strict gain missing from separate worst-case incidence bounds. The scaling threshold depends on each fixed graph, cover, and field vector. The known persistent-failure bracket improves to $[9,38]$; the finite-activity bracket remains $[6,38]$. General finite-activity ultra-log-concavity at ranks six through eight is not proved.

\subsection{Main theorem and the remaining cover split}
For a finite bipartite graph $G=(X,Y)$, let
\[
 P_G(t)=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq X,\ J\subseteq Y\\|I|=|J|,\ G[I,J]\text{ perfectly matchable}}}u_Iv_Jt^{|I|}.
\]
Each feasible endpoint pair is counted once, rather than once per matching. If the matching rank is $d$ and $P_G=\sum p_jt^j$, its actual-rank gaps are
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:gaps}
 G_j=j(d-j)p_j^2-(j+1)(d-j+1)p_{j-1}p_{j+1}.
\end{equation}
The condition $P_G\in\ULC_d$ means that these gaps are nonnegative and there are no internal zeros.

Fix a minimum cover $A\sqcup B$, with $A\subseteq X$, $B\subseteq Y$, and put $L=X\setminus A$, $R=Y\setminus B$. Set every left activity to one. Fix a vector $v\in(0,\infty)^Y$, and replace $v_y$ by $\lambda v_y$ for $y\in B$ only.

\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:e:r8:thm:global}
If $d\le8$, there is a finite threshold $\lambda_0=\lambda_0(G,A,B,v)$ such that $P_G\in\ULC_d$ for every $\lambda\ge\lambda_0$.
\end{theorem}
The theorem is globally nonstrict: pure-shore and small-cover branches can have equality. No uniform threshold over graphs or fields is claimed.

The sealed companions \cite{b:m03,b:m02} settle all rank-eight cover splits except the middle conditional comparison for $4+4$. Henceforth let $|A|=|B|=4$, and remove isolated exterior vertices. Exterior ranks are four on both $L$--$B$ and $A$--$R$. Forcing all $B$ vertices and dividing out their activities gives
\[
 t^4C(t),\qquad C(t)=\sum_{j=0}^4C_jt^j,\qquad C_j>0.
\]
The required conditional factors are $8/5$, $7/4$, and $16/7$. The first is below the ambient matroid bound, and the last is strict by \cite{b:m02}. We settle the middle factor.

\subsection{The auxiliary quartic and a sufficient unit potential}
Use independent indeterminates for graph edges, so square-minor nonvanishing is exactly perfect matchability. Let $q\ge1$ count independent four-subsets of exterior $L$. For each independent labeled $L$ triple, introduce the core image of its annihilator as a virtual right point, keeping loops and parallel labels. Adjoin the four private $A$ coordinate vectors. The resulting rank-four matroid has a Lorentzian basis polynomial \cite{b:BH}. Mark private, original $R$, and virtual elements by $s,t,z$, with the actual positive activities on $R$. Set
\[
 f(t,u)=F(qu,t,u),\qquad b_j=[t^ju^{4-j}]f.
\]
The graph-generic upper incidence theorem of \cite{b:m02} gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:auxupper}
 b_2\le qC_2,\qquad b_3=C_3.
\end{equation}
Quartic Newton therefore proves the desired strict middle gap once
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:suffices}
 b_1>\frac79q^2C_1.
\end{equation}
Indeed $b_2^2\ge(9/4)b_1b_3$ then yields $C_2^2>(7/4)C_1C_3$.

Fix one exterior $R$ vector and quotient by it. The quotient has dimension three; its private images form a spanning configuration $E$ of three or four nonloop labeled elements, all with activity one. Let $V$ count its bases and $K$ be the induced $3\times4$ core map. Write $X$ for the one-virtual, two-private basis count, $N$ for the two-original-$L$, one-private endpoint sector, and $Z$ for the one-original-$L$ sector. The exact fixed-vector contribution to $C_1$ is
\[
 C=qV+X+N+Z.
\]
Let $E_2$ count two-virtual, one-private bases and $F_3$ virtual triples. The corresponding auxiliary contribution is
\[
 b=q^3V+q^2X+qE_2+F_3.
\]
The universal rank-four plane incidence bound gives $E_2\ge(2/3)qN$. The next section proves $F_3\ge(2/5)q^2Z$ in the graph-generic setting. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:potential}
 9b-7q^2C\ge\frac{q^2}{5}\mathcal P,
 \qquad \mathcal P=10qV+10X-5N-17Z.
\end{equation}
If the core rank is below three, both $F_3$ and $Z$ vanish. We will prove $\mathcal P>0$ for the cases requiring this comparison and handle the remaining parallel-class cases exactly.

\subsection{A finite hyperplane transition bound}
Let $H_3$ be a fixed three-plane independent of the graph-generic rank-four rows $L$, with normal $n_H$. Let $Z$ count original elements outside $H_3$. For an independent triple $P$ with $\spn(P)\ne H_3$, let $\beta_{P}$ count original pairs complementary to $\spn(P)\cap H_3$. With arbitrary positive original activities, define
\[
 S=\sum_P w_P\beta_P.
\]

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:r8:lem:transition}
Coefficientwise in the original activities,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:transition}
 5S\ge9qZ.
\end{equation}
Consequently the virtual triple count after restriction to $H_3$ satisfies $F_3\ge(2/5)q^2Z$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A term of $S$ uses a triple and a pair. If they share two labels, the contribution vanishes. If they share one label $a$, the complement determinant is nonzero exactly when their four-label union is a basis and $a\notin H_3$. There are three choices of the marked triple and pair, giving coefficients three in $S$ and one in $qZ$.

For five distinct labels, nonempty row supports are masks $1,\ldots,15$. Normalize the nonzero entries of $n_H$ to one. Up to coordinate permutation its support is $1$, $3$, $7$, or $15$. For a triple $P$ and its complementary pair $i,j$, the determinant is, up to sign,
\[
 \det(P,\ell_i)(n_H\cdot\ell_j)
 -\det(P,\ell_j)(n_H\cdot\ell_i).
\]
A monomial fixes the missing coordinate $a$ of the triple. If both pair rows choose $a$, the terms cancel. Otherwise one pair coordinate is $a$, the other is a distinct normal-supported coordinate, and exactly one term contributes. Thus a split is counted precisely when a possible missing coordinate of the triple and a distinct normal-supported coordinate can be matched by the complementary pair. This is an exact support test, independent of the nonzero values of the fixed normal.

For every nondecreasing five-row profile, let $Q$ count outside-row deletions leaving a basis, and let $S_5$ count these ten triple-pair splits. The complete integer table checks $5S_5\ge9Q$ on all
\[
 4\binom{19}{5}=46{,}512
\]
profiles. Two independently implemented coordinate-injection replays verify the same four profile hashes. The stronger coefficientwise assertion $S_5\ge2Q$ has 184 exceptions for support $3$ and 120 for support $7$, none for the other two supports; every exception has $(S_5,Q)=(9,5)$. Thus the weaker constant in \eqref{mrn:e:r8:eq:transition} is deliberate.

Finally mark one virtual point in a virtual basis. The universal rank-four lower plane bound, applied to $\spn(P)\cap H_3$, gives
\[
 3F_3\ge\frac23qS\ge\frac65q^2Z.
\]
Here the intersected plane may depend on original rows, but this lower incidence theorem is universal for represented configurations. Dividing by three proves the result.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Two elementary minor count lemmas}
For a matrix $M$, let $m_i(M)$ count its nonzero minors of size $i$.

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:r8:lem:minor}
Every rank-three matrix satisfies
\[
 m_3+m_2\ge m_1-2,\qquad
 \Phi(M):=2m_3+2m_2-m_1\ge5.
\]
Adding a nonzero row or column while retaining rank three increases $\Phi$ by at least three. In particular, a rank-three matrix with at least four nonzero rows or columns has $\Phi\ge8$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Start with an invertible $3\times3$ submatrix. If its adjugate has at least six nonzero entries, $m_3=1$, $m_2\ge6$, $m_1\le9$ prove both bounds. If the adjugate has three, four, or five entries, permute a perfect matching onto its diagonal and scale those entries to one. It is the identity plus at most two off-diagonal entries. Its inverse has at most three, four, or six entries, respectively: two arrows give either a two-cycle or at most one extra length-two path. The bounds follow in each case.

Suppose a new row has $z$ nonzero entries. Represent the old columns as spanning vectors $x_j$ in dimension three and the new row as a functional $\ell$. For each $j$ with $\ell(x_j)\ne0$, the differences
\[
 x_k-\frac{\ell(x_k)}{\ell(x_j)}x_j
\]
span the two-dimensional kernel of $\ell$. Two distinct $k$ therefore give nonzero two-minors with column $j$ and suitable old rows. Each new two-minor is counted at most twice over $j$, so at least $z$ new two-minors appear. A nonzero added row also creates at least one three-minor, since the old row space has rank three. Hence $m_3+m_2-m_1$ cannot decrease, and
\[
 \Delta\Phi\ge2+z\ge3.
\]
Transpose for columns. Zero added rows or columns change none of the counts. Starting from the chosen invertible submatrix and adding all remaining rows and columns proves the assertions.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:r8:lem:rectangular}
Every rank-three $3\times4$ matrix satisfies $m_2\le2m_1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Transpose to four rows with three coordinates. If a row is zero, use the remaining invertible $3\times3$ matrix: $m_1\ge5$ gives $m_2\le9\le2m_1$, while $m_1=3,4$ gives $m_2=3,4$.

If all four nonzero rows have support at least two, $m_1\ge8$. At $m_1=8$, two supports coincide. Their pair contributes at most one two-minor, and the other five pairs at most fifteen. At $m_1\ge9$, use $m_2\le18$.

Suppose one row has singleton support. Its three pairs contribute at most $m_1-1$ two-minors, and the other three at most nine. Thus $m_2\le m_1+8$, enough for $m_1\ge8$. For smaller $m_1$, the remaining support-size patterns give the following bounds: $(1,1,1)$ at $m_1=4$ gives $3+3=6$; $(2,1,1)$ at five gives $4+5=9$; $(3,1,1)$ and $(2,2,1)$ at six give $5+5=10$ and $5+7=12$; $(3,2,1)$ at seven gives $6+7=13$. For $(2,2,2)$, if a remaining row uses the singleton coordinate, the first three pairs contribute at most five and the other three at most nine. Otherwise those three rows lie in the other coordinate plane and their mutual pairs contribute at most three. Every bound is at most $2m_1$.
\end{proof}
An independent exhaustive check of the 330 unordered four-column support patterns, including zero columns, gives a second finite verification of Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:r8:lem:rectangular}. Generic support counts upper-bound the actual nonzero two-minor count of every realization with that support.

\subsection{The starting potential and its increments}
We work in one fixed-$R$ quotient from Section 2 \mnote{Subsection~\ref{mrn:src:01}.2 here}. Begin with four original $L$ rows forming a basis, so $q=1$ and the four virtual images are the four core columns after an invertible basis change.

First use three private basis elements. If the core has rank three, $X,N,Z$ count the nonzero entries, two-minors, and three-minors of this $3\times4$ matrix. Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:r8:lem:rectangular} gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:start}
 \mathcal P\ge10-17Z.
\end{equation}
Here $Z$ is the number of the four original basis rows outside the row-space hyperplane $H_3$ of the core.

If the core has rank two, let $W\in\{1,2,3\}$ count private basis elements outside its image plane, $k\le4$ the number of nonzero virtual images, and $p$ their independent-pair count. Then $N=Wp$. For $W=1$, $p\le\binom{k}{2}\le3k/2$ and $X\ge k$. For $W=2$, let $k_0$ count virtual points in the one private direction contained in the image plane, and $k_1$ the others. Then
\[
 X\ge k_0+2k_1,\qquad p\le k_0k_1+\binom{k_1}{2},\qquad k_0+k_1\le4,
\]
which implies $p\le X$. For $W=3$, $X\ge2k$ and $N\le3\binom{k}{2}\le2(1+X)$. In every case $2(1+X)\ge N$, so $\mathcal P\ge0$. At smaller core rank, $N=Z=0$.

Adding a possible fourth nonloop private element cannot lower the starting potential. Quotient by it. The old private images span dimension two, with private-pair count $\Delta V\ge1$. Let $\Delta X$ be their mixed basis count with the four marked virtual images, and $\Delta N$ the marked-pair count. If $\Delta N>0$, two independent marked and two independent private images give $\Delta X\ge2$, whereas $\Delta N\le6$. Thus $2\Delta V+2\Delta X\ge\Delta N$, proving $\Delta\mathcal P\ge0$. The value of $Z$ is unchanged. Hence \eqref{mrn:e:r8:eq:start}, or $\mathcal P\ge0$ at core rank below three, holds for the actual private configuration.

Now add a nonzero original row $\ell$ to a spanning old configuration. Quotienting the old original rows by $\ell$ gives a rank-three configuration after omitting loops. Write $q',X',N'$ for the increments of $q,X,N$.

If the core has rank three and $\ell\notin H_3$, its restriction to the annihilator plane of $\ell$ is invertible. In suitable coordinates the transformed old-row/private matrix $M$ has rank three, and
\[
 (m_3(M),m_2(M),m_1(M))=(q'V,X',N').
\]
These are the same complementary-minor identities used for the endpoint sectors. Also $\Delta Z=1$. Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:r8:lem:minor} gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:outside}
 \Delta\mathcal P=5\Phi(M)-17\ge8.
\end{equation}
If at least four old rows survive as nonloops modulo $\ell$, then $\Phi(M)\ge8$ and
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:strongoutside}
 \Delta\mathcal P\ge23.
\end{equation}

For an inside row, the restricted core has rank two. More generally this is the only nontrivial increment at smaller core rank. We claim
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:e:r8:eq:insideunit}
 2q'V+2X'-N'\ge1,
 \qquad \Delta\mathcal P\ge5.
\end{equation}
At restricted rank at most one, $N'=0$ and $q'V\ge1$. At rank two, let $n'\ge3$ count nonloop quotient rows, let $W$ count private images outside the core image plane, and let $\beta\le n'$ be their common outside-row count. Then
\[
 N'=W\beta,\qquad q'\ge n'-2,\qquad 2X'\ge W(n'-1).
\]
The last inequality counts a good virtual point and a first private element, completes by a second private element, and counts each resulting private-pair basis at most twice. With three private elements $V=1$; with four, $V\ge2$. Consequently
\[
 2q'V+2X'-N'\ge2(n'-2)V-W.
\]
This is at least one except for the following boundary cases at $n'=3$.

For three private basis elements and $W=2$, if $\beta\le2$, then $X'\ge2$ gives a margin at least two. If $\beta=3$, the three nonzero virtual images have three distinct directions. At most one is parallel to the private direction in the image plane; the others have at least two private coordinates, so $X'\ge5$. For $W=3$, every nonzero virtual image has at least two coordinates. There are at least two such images, so $X'\ge4$, giving margin at least one.

For four private elements, the only remaining case is $V=2,W=4$. The private configuration is a parallel pair and two coloops. No nonzero virtual image in the core plane is parallel to a private element. In a private basis it has support at least two, and the duplicated coordinate ensures at least three private-pair completions. There are at least two nonzero virtual images, so $X'\ge6$ and the margin is at least four. This proves \eqref{mrn:e:r8:eq:insideunit} in every case.

\subsection{Parallel classes and the strict middle gap}
We first record the exact case that cannot be disposed of by coarse growth bounds.

\begin{lemma}\label{mrn:e:r8:lem:parallel}
For an $a+b$ cover with unit $A$ activities, suppose the exterior rank-$b$ configuration has exactly $b$ independent parallel classes of positive masses $m_i$. Let $q=\prod_i m_i$. For its conditional coefficients $C_j$ and the corresponding auxiliary specialization with private activity $q$, one has
\[
 b_j=q^{a-j-1}C_j\qquad(0\le j\le a).
\]
In particular the conditional polynomial is $\ULC_a$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Normalize the class directions to a basis. The virtual cofactor class omitting original class $i$ has total activity $q/m_i$. A selected missing-class set $T$ has virtual weight $\prod_{i\in T}q/m_i$. Its auxiliary basis uses $a-j-|T|$ private elements, giving total weight
\[
 \frac{q^{a-j}}{\prod_{i\in T}m_i}.
\]
The complementary original $L$ support has weight $q/\prod_{i\in T}m_i$. Complementary subsets have exactly the same nonvanishing condition after core elimination, including loops and any further parallel identifications. Summing gives the stated coefficient identity. The auxiliary polynomial is Lorentzian; its one-variable coefficients are $\ULC_a$. Geometric rescaling preserves this property, proving the claim.
\end{proof}
For $4+4$, this gives $b_1=q^2C_1$, $b_2=qC_2$ and the stronger middle factor $9/4$. Since $C_1C_3>0$, it is strictly stronger than $7/4$. The lemma applies to the faithful generic graph representation; arbitrary nongeneric numerical matrices are not substituted for graph endpoint supports.

Suppose now that $n=|L|\ge9$ and there are at least five nonloop parallel classes. If the fixed-$R$ core has rank at most two, its starting potential is nonnegative and every added row raises it by at least five, so $\mathcal P>0$.

At core rank three, put $r=\rk(L\cap H_3)$. Choose a starting basis with $r$ inside and $4-r$ outside rows. If $r\ge2$, \eqref{mrn:e:r8:eq:start} and the increment five give
\[
 \mathcal P\ge10-17(4-r)+5(n-4)>0;
\]
the worst endpoint is $r=2,n=9$, with value one.

If $r=0$, all rows are outside. Choose a basis and then a row in a fifth parallel class. All four old rows survive modulo it, so its increment is at least 23. Thereafter contracting any new row kills at most one of the at least five old parallel classes, leaving at least four nonloop rows. Every later increment is also at least 23. Hence
\[
 \mathcal P\ge-58+23(n-4)>0
\]
for $n\ge7$, in particular throughout the needed range.

If $r=1$, all inside rows form one parallel class. At least four outside classes exist. Choose one inside and three outside rows as a basis, and then an outside fourth-class row. That one increment is at least 23; all later increments are at least five. Thus
\[
 \mathcal P\ge-41+23+5(n-5)>0
\]
at $n\ge9$, with endpoint value two.

We have proved $\mathcal P>0$ in every required fixed-$R$ quotient, or handled the four-class case exactly. Summing \eqref{mrn:e:r8:eq:potential} with the positive exterior-right activities gives \eqref{mrn:e:r8:eq:suffices}. By \eqref{mrn:e:r8:eq:auxupper} and quartic Newton,
\[
 C_2^2\ge\frac{b_2^2}{q^2}\ge\frac94\frac{b_1C_3}{q^2}
 >\frac74C_1C_3.
\]
Thus the middle gap is strict for every genuine $4+4$ cover with $n\ge9$.

\subsection{The lower population boundary and eventual scaling}
The ambient transversal matroid after contracting $B$ has degree $n$. Its normalized factor at conditional index two is
\[
 \frac{3(n-1)}{2(n-2)},
\]
strictly above $7/4$ for $n\le7$ and equal at $n=8$.

At $n=8$, use the weighted local equality criterion proved in the structural companion bundled with \cite{b:m03} \mnote{source~08s's Lemma~\ref{mrn:e:tg:lem:strict}, which is the necessity half of source~72's Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:se:thm:weighted}}: equality at index two forces compatible positive red and blue masses on every nonloop parallel class of every rank-two contraction by one red and five blue elements. In particular, a contraction retaining two independent blue elements and a red-only nonloop class forces strictness.

Choose an exterior $L$ basis. After contracting $B$, the blue basis consists of the four private $A$ coordinates and four complementary private $L$ coordinates. If an $R$ vertex $u$ has two $A$ neighbors, choose a matching extending $u$ first, name its assigned coordinate $i$, and choose another neighbor $j$. From the other two coordinates choose a matched red element $R_h$. Contract it, the private $A$ coordinate at the other remaining position, and all four complementary private $L$ coordinates. This independent six-set has one red and five blue elements. The surviving $A_i,A_j$ remain independent, while $u$ has two free edge coefficients independent of the contracted and blue data. Its quotient line is parallel to no nonloop blue element. The local criterion therefore makes the middle gap strict.

If every exterior $R$ vertex is a singleton leaf, compress its four classes to four vertices of summed activity. The whole right shore then has eight vertices, and the pure-shore theorem gives $\ULC_8$ at every positive field. This branch needs no strictness assertion.

For all other cases the conditional first and top gaps are already strict by \cite{b:m02}, and the middle gap is now strict. The low-index leading terms under common $B$ scaling are those of the pure right-shore-$B$ support polynomial, whose degree-four normalization is stronger than the actual-rank-eight normalization. At index four, the square term has higher scaling degree than the neighboring product. At higher indices the strict leading coefficients are exactly the conditional gaps proved above. Each gap polynomial is therefore positive for sufficiently large $\lambda$.

The sealed $5+3$ theorem \cite{b:m03}, its earlier $3+5$ input, the at-most-two-cover theorem, and the rank-seven result bundled there exhaust the other cover splits at ranks at most eight. This proves Theorem~\ref{mrn:e:r8:thm:global}, retaining unit activities on the entire left shore throughout.

For a strict branch with gap $G(\lambda)=a_D\lambda^D+\sum_{i<D}a_i\lambda^i$, $a_D>0$, a sufficient explicit field-dependent threshold is
\[
 \lambda\ge1+\frac{\sum_{i<D}|a_i|}{a_D}.
\]
Take the maximum over its finitely many gaps. Pure-shore and small-cover branches already satisfy ULC at every positive field. No finite-activity theorem for general rank-six, rank-seven or rank-eight graphs is inferred.

\subsection{Failure ranks and verification scope}
The unchanged rank-38 witness in the bundled prerequisites has blocks $|A|=8$, $|B|=30$, $|L|=32$, $|R|=794$, and edges $(A\times B)\cup(L\times B)\cup(A\times R)$. All left and exterior-right activities are one, while every $B$ activity is $w$. Its last gap is
\[
 G_{37}=\frac5{6304}D^2w^{58}
 \bigl(-22w^2+6437219280w+11385894392295\bigr),
 \qquad D=\binom{32}{30}\binom{794}{7}.
\]
It is negative for all sufficiently large $w$. Theorem~\ref{mrn:e:r8:thm:global} therefore improves the persistent common-scaling failure-rank bracket to $[9,38]$. The separate finite-activity bracket stays $[6,38]$. \mnote{Its lower end rests on source~74 (Part~I); its upper end on source~55 (Part~II). The persistent-failure bracket $[9,38]$ is the final one of the batch; sources~21 and~08s gave $[7,38]$ and source~08 $[8,38]$.}

The new finite proof input is the complete $46{,}512$-profile hyperplane table. Its independent coordinate-injection replay agrees with every coefficient and all four hashes. The minor-count growth lemmas and parallel-class identity are elementary proofs, not bounded-population checks; the 330 support-pattern verification supplements the rectangular lemma. All mathematical checks in the portable scripts use exact arithmetic and explicit failures, so they remain effective under optimized Python.

The two preceding PDFs and source archives are included unchanged. They supply the previously cleared graph-generic upper incidence, strict top and $5+3$ comparisons, Lorentzian sector construction, small-cover theorem and weighted local equality criterion. This report and its certificates are ordinary mathematical proofs and exact computation, not proof-assistant certification or external refereeing. No exhaustive priority claim is made.

\part{Second routes and boundaries}\label{mrn:part:five}

\paragraph{Contents of Part~V.}
Part~V prints four manuscripts of batch~73 of ProveIt's intake (arrival
commit \texttt{f8c3a392a}, placement commit \texttt{5e4f1263c}). They are
numbered 77--80 here, continuing this report's batch-72 numbers; their
batch-73 manuscript numbers are 26, 08, 02 and~01. All four work in Part~I's
setting: independent vertex activities on both shores, each pair of endpoint
sets counted once, normalization by the actual matching number. They were
written in parallel with batch~72: source~77
(\path{matching-rank-four-package.zip}) on the evening of 30 September 2026,
sources~78 and~79 early on 1 October, all three before batch~72 arrived, and
source~80 after its arrival (its latest file is dated less than an hour
later). Source~80 alone read batch-72
material, the raw archives of sources~74, 10 and~05. None of them read this
report or Part~XIII of \prp. Most of what
they prove is therefore already in Parts~I and~III or in \prp; such results
keep their statements, and their proofs are printed as marked second routes
or replaced by pointers. New in Part~V:
\begin{itemize}
\item source~77 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:77}): at unit activities and matching
  number three, a non-real-rooted support polynomial needs fifteen vertices,
  and the fifteen-vertex example is unique
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:thm:minimum}, computer-assisted), with the
  pair-neighbourhood family behind it;
\item source~78 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:78}): a real-stable seed for a
  $2+3$ cover whose core is a star centred at a vertex of the two-vertex
  part with exactly two neighbours in the three-vertex part (the exceptional
  two-neighbour seed in Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:st:thm:star});
\item source~79 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:79}): a two-element Lorentzian
  deformation of every matroid basis polynomial
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:te:thm:main}), with two proofs, the first from
  source~80's appendix;
\item source~80 (Section~\ref{mrn:src:80}): a positive semidefinite
  first-layer theorem for any number of left vertices
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD}), which proves source~10's three-vertex
  marginal theorem without its 5339-monomial certificate, and three
  thirteen-vertex obstructions showing that Part~I's two-tail Lorentzian
  lifts do not extend to three tails.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Same results in Parts~I--IV and in \prp.}
Source~77's weighted rank-four theorem is source~76's
Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:sb:cor:rankfour} and a case of source~74's
Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive}; its real-rootedness theorem for a
$2+2$ cover is the univariate consequence of source~76's
Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:sb:thm:main}, and its elementary proof (weighted paths,
Hermitian Gram determinants, a positive semidefinite block matrix) is
printed as a second route; its thin-cover reduction is Part~XIII of \prp{}
(pointers); its $1+2$ lemma is \prp's Theorem~207.1 (proof printed as a
second route, because the rank-three minimum uses it); its strict rank-five
family is a case of source~75's Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:fh:thm:classification}
(certificate route printed). Source~78's theorem for $2+3$ cores is a case of
Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive} with source~72's strictness; its 26
certificates are printed as a computer-assisted second route. Source~79's
graph corollaries are source~74's Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:main} and
Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive}. Source~80's scalar counterexamples are
Part~XI's Theorem~177.1 of \prp{} and source~74's
Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:negative} (displays kept, derivations pointers), and
its three-tail cubic is source~10's Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:tv:thm:marginal}
(second route). Five further batch-73 manuscripts on the same object are
duplicates and are not printed (Section~\ref{mrn:sec:sources}).

\paragraph{Notation of Part~V.}
Sources~78--80 write the shores as $P\sqcup X$ and $Q\sqcup Y$: $P$ and $Q$
are the two parts of the displayed cover (Part~I's $A$ and $B$) and $X$, $Y$
the exterior sets (Part~I's $L$ and $R$). Here $X$ and $Y$ are \emph{not}
whole shores, as they are elsewhere in this report. Source~77 writes
$G=(L,R,E)$: there $L$ and $R$ \emph{are} the whole shores, not exterior
sets. ``Tail'' and ``head'' mean left and right vertex; ``actual'' or
``surviving'' degree is the matching number after zero-activity vertices are
deleted; $\Gamma(t)=\sum\gamma_kt^k$ (sources~78, 80) is $p_G$. Source~79's
distinguished matroid elements $A,B$ are not cover parts, and source~80
writes $N$ for an exterior population size in
Section~\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:scalar} but for a matrix in
Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD}. The table of Section~\ref{mrn:sec:notions}
lists these readings.

\paragraph{Non-claims of Part~V.}
Source~77's minimum concerns unit activities and matching number exactly three
only; it is computer-assisted and makes no claim across matching numbers
(\prp's theta graph has eight vertices) and no priority claim; its rank-five
theorem covers block-constant activities only. Source~78's theorem is
computer-assisted, its complete-exterior hypothesis is essential, its full core
can have nonreal zeros, and the two-tail/three-head role cover stays open.
Source~79 makes no joint Lorentzian assertion in its population parameters,
identifies no physical tail and head roles, and leaves the $3+3$ cover outside.
Source~80's obstructions concern multivariate homogenizations whose scalar
sequences remain $\ULC$; it gives no unweighted counterexample and no
minimum-vertex bound, and Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD} does not assert full
Lorentzianity for more than three tails. No source of Part~V claims global
priority, referee review or formalization.

%% ======================== source 77 ========================
\section{Weighted rank four, real roots for two-by-two covers, and the rank-three minimum (source~77)}\label{mrn:src:77}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~77, as delivered.} We prove actual-degree ultra-log-concavity for weighted matching-support polynomials of every bipartite graph with matching number at most four. Each pair of equal-size shore subsets admitting a perfect matching is counted once, with product vertex activities; arbitrary nonnegative activities are allowed on both shores. A minimum-cover reduction combines pendant compression and the credited ambient Lorentzian theorem with exact weighted-path and Hermitian determinant constructions. For covers split as two vertices on each shore, a stronger real-rootedness theorem holds; the complete core is handled by a positive-semidefinite block matrix. General rank-four support polynomials need not be real-rooted. The result answers the inspected report's weighted rank-three and rank-four questions. Two extensions give an exact computer-assisted classification of the smallest unit-weight non-real-rooted example at matching rank three, and strict rank-five normalization for a specified complete-core family with four constant activity blocks. General rank five and the unit-weight all-rank question remain open.
\mnote{Source~77 is batch-73 manuscript~26, revision~2 of
\path{matching-rank-four-package.zip} (title page ``Research note prepared
with OpenAI for Vladimir Reshetnikov''). Its first, twelve-page edition,
batch-73 archive~27 (\texttt{matching-rank-four-package~(1).zip}), contains no
statement, proof or example that is not printed here and is not a source
(\path{77-rank-four-REVISION.md}). Source~77 inspected \prp{} at commit
\texttt{d5e863bba}, before \prp's Part~XIII and before this report existed.
Its Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:thm:main} is source~76's
Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:sb:cor:rankfour}, the case $\nu\le4$ of source~74's
Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive}. Its
Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:thm:mixed} is the univariate consequence of source~76's
Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:sb:thm:main}, which proves real stability of the basis
polynomial as well; source~77's proof
(Sections~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:sec:path}--\ref{mrn:v:rf4:sec:full}) is elementary and
different, and is printed as a second route to that theorem's univariate
conclusion. New here are the family $p_n$ of
Section~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:sec:rank3-example}, the twin remark and
Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:thm:minimum}.}

\subsection{Support weights and the main theorem}
Let $G=(L,R,E)$ be a finite simple bipartite graph. A pair $(I,J)$ with $I\subseteq L$, $J\subseteq R$, and $|I|=|J|$ is \emph{matchable} if $G[I,J]$ has a perfect matching. For activities $u_x,v_y\ge0$, define
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:rf4:eq:support}
p_G(t)=\sum_{k\ge0}a_kt^k,
\qquad
a_k=\sum_{\substack{I\subseteq L,\ J\subseteq R\\|I|=|J|=k\\G[I,J]\text{ matchable}}}
\prod_{x\in I}u_x\prod_{y\in J}v_y.
\end{equation}
The empty support has weight one. A support with several perfect matchings contributes \emph{one} product of activities, not one term for each matching. This differs from the ordinary matching generating polynomial.

Write $\nu(G)$ for the maximum matching size and $d=\deg p_G$. The degree is the matching number of the induced graph on positive-activity vertices, after isolated vertices are ignored. Indeed its largest matching supplies a positive degree-$d$ support, and a support cannot exist above its matching number. Submatchings show that $a_0,\ldots,a_d$ are all positive.
\begin{theorem}[Actual-degree normalization through rank four]\label{mrn:v:rf4:thm:main}
If $\nu(G)\le4$, then for every choice of nonnegative two-shore vertex activities, the sequence
\[
\left(\frac{a_k}{\binom d k}\right)_{k=0}^d
\]
is log-concave and has no internal zeros. Equivalently, for $1\le k<d$,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:rf4:eq:newton}
k(d-k)a_k^2\ge(k+1)(d-k+1)a_{k-1}a_{k+1}.
\end{equation}
For $d=0$ or $1$ the assertion has no interior inequality.
\end{theorem}
\mnote{This is source~76's Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:sb:cor:rankfour} and the
case $\nu\le4$ of source~74's Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive}, both
including nonnegative activities with the surviving degree. Source~77's proof
(end of Section~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:sec:completion}) is a further route.}


The structural statement used for the remaining mixed cover is stronger.
\begin{theorem}[Real roots for a two by two cover]\label{mrn:v:rf4:thm:mixed}
If $G$ has a vertex cover consisting of two left vertices and two right vertices, then $p_G$ has only negative real zeros, for arbitrary nonnegative activities. Its degree may be smaller than four. In particular it satisfies \eqref{mrn:v:rf4:eq:newton} at its actual degree.
\end{theorem}
\mnote{Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:thm:mixed} is the univariate conclusion of
source~76's Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:sb:thm:main} (real stability of the
complemented-row basis polynomial for a $2+2$ cover). Its proof here is a
second route to that conclusion only; it does not give multivariate
stability.}


At $d=4$, the three inequalities are
\[
3a_1^2\ge8a_2,\qquad4a_2^2\ge9a_1a_3,\qquad
3a_3^2\ge8a_2a_4.
\]
The inspected ProveIt manuscript proves the unweighted result through rank three, asks for the two remaining rank-four inequalities, and separately asks for arbitrary two-shore weights at rank three \cite[questions \texttt{mr:q:rankfour}, \texttt{mr:q:weighted}, and \texttt{hb:q:rank}]{b:PRP}. Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:thm:main} answers these finite-rank questions, including the rank-three and rank-four parts of the last one. It does not claim all-rank weighted normalization: that same manuscript already exhibits a weighted rank-six counterexample.
\mnote{The three questions are Research questions~93 (\path{mr:q:rankfour}),
95 (\path{mr:q:weighted}) and~86 (\path{hb:q:rank}) of \prp. They were open at
source~77's pin \texttt{d5e863bba}. \prp{} now records them as answered: weighted
rank three by its Part~XIII (Corollary~206.3), and ranks four and five, with
the least weighted failure rank exactly six, by source~74 in Part~I. Source~77's
answer is a further route, not the first one.}


The ambient Lorentzian theorem, transversal-matroid correspondence, Newton inequalities, spectral theorem, Cauchy--Binet expansion, and matching--cover theorem are established inputs or classical machinery. The paper gives the finite-rank reductions and matrix constructions in detail and makes no global priority claim. No proof-assistant formalization is asserted.

\subsection{The ambient theorem and the cover reduction}\label{mrn:v:rf4:sec:ambient}

We use the weighted ambient normalization exactly with its legitimate parameter.
\begin{proposition}[Weighted smaller-shore normalization]\label{mrn:v:rf4:prop:ambient}
For positive activities on a bipartite graph with shore sizes $m,n$, the sequence $a_k/\binom{s}{k}$, $0\le k\le s$, is log-concave after zero padding, where $s=\min(m,n)$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof (pointer)]
This is Theorem~70.1 of \prp{} (Part~V, \path{lor:thm:weightedULC}),
Lemma~\ref{mrn:w:rf:lem:small} here. \mnote{Source~77 proves it by the
private-slot transversal matroid on $L^\circ\sqcup R$ with slots $L$, whose
bases are $(L^\circ\setminus I^\circ)\sqcup J$ for matchable $(I,J)$, the
substitution $s/u_x$, $tv_y$ and the bivariate characterization of
Lorentzian polynomials \cite[Theorems 3.10 and 2.10, Example 2.26]{b:BH}.
That is \prp's proof; source~77 credits it, and the unweighted
relation-bimatroid case to R\"ohrle--Ulirsch \cite[Theorem A and Example
2.4]{b:RU}. It is not reprinted.}
\end{proof}


One cannot replace $m$ in this homogeneous lift by $\nu(G)$ merely by cancelling a power of $s$. Such cancellation does not generally preserve the Lorentzian property. The reductions below arrange a genuinely smaller shore or construct a separate real-rooted polynomial.

\begin{lemma}[Pendant compression]\label{mrn:v:rf4:lem:pendant}
Suppose $P\subseteq R$ is a nonempty set of vertices with common neighborhood $\{a\}$ for some $a\in L$. Replace $P$ by one vertex with neighborhood $\{a\}$ and activity $\sum_{y\in P}v_y$. This preserves $p_G$ and the matching number.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[Proof (pointer)]
This is weighted leaf compression, Lemma~206.1 of \prp{} (Part~XIII,
\path{lc:lem:compression}). \mnote{Source~77's proof is the same: a feasible
support contains at most one vertex of $P$, and the supports through $P$ for
fixed $(I,J_0)$ are all matchable or none is. It is not reprinted.}
\end{proof}


\begin{proposition}[Covers with a small shore part]\label{mrn:v:rf4:prop:thin}
With positive activities, if a minimum cover of size $r$ has at most one vertex on one shore, then $p_G$ satisfies \eqref{mrn:v:rf4:eq:newton} with $d=r$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof (pointer)]
This is Theorem~206.2 of \prp{} (Part~XIII, \path{lc:thm:singleton}),
Lemma~\ref{mrn:w:rf:lem:singleton} here. \mnote{Source~77's proof is the same
argument: transpose so that one cover part has at most one vertex, compress the
pendant vertices at that vertex by Lemma~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:lem:pendant}, and apply
Proposition~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:prop:ambient} to the resulting shore of exactly $r$
vertices. It is not reprinted.}
\end{proof}


K\H{o}nig's classical matching--cover theorem provides a cover of size $\nu(G)$.
