The bivariate characterization \cite[Example 2.26]{b:BH} gives log-concavity of $\gamma_k/\binom{q+2}{k}$. This is cover-order normalization; arbitrary degree drops do not by themselves permit replacement of $q+2$ by the surviving degree.

\begin{corollary}\label{mrn:v:te:cor:rankfive}
For every independently nonnegative vertex-weighted finite bipartite graph whose surviving matching rank is $d\le5$, its endpoint-support sequence is ultra-log-concave normalized by $\binom d k$.
\end{corollary}
\mnote{This is source~74's Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive} (Part~I), with
the same reduction by K\H{o}nig's theorem and leaf compression; the proof below
is a second route through Corollary~\ref{mrn:v:te:cor:graph}.}

\begin{proof}
Delete zero-activity vertices and ignore isolates. By K\H{o}nig's matching-cover theorem \cite{b:Konig}, choose a vertex cover of size $d$, and reverse the shores so that at most two cover vertices are left vertices. For two, use Corollary~\ref{mrn:v:te:cor:graph} with $q=d-2$. For zero, the ordinary right-dummy transversal basis construction gives a degree-$d$ Lorentzian homogenization. For one left cover vertex $p$ and $q=d-1$ right cover vertices, every nonisolated outside-right vertex has the same sole neighbor $p$. At most one can be selected, so replace them by one head of activity equal to their total. The rank-$(q+1)$ transversal basis polynomial with private head dummies gives the same homogenized support polynomial. An artificial zero-activity head, interpreted by the same coefficient limit, handles an empty population. Thus in every case the homogenization order is exactly $d$. The empty graph is immediate; the bivariate characterization proves the claim.
\end{proof}

The stronger multivariate conclusion keeps the left selected variables, but still treats $Y$ activities as parameters. No identification of physical tail and head roles in a general directed relation follows. The balanced three-plus-three cover case at actual rank six is also outside the present theorem.

\subsection{Proof status and reproducibility}
The proof uses only the cited matroid and Lorentzian facts and the displayed identities. No exhaustive or randomized finite search is a premise. The accompanying package contains four exact corroborating checkers: the producer checks 60 represented matroids, 3,505 rational Hessian inertias and 94,442 support exchanges; a separate represented-matroid checker verifies 250 literal augmentation identities and 1,240 contractions; an abstract rank-oracle checker verifies 84 principal-line identities, 840 matroid-union identities over 82,331 candidate bases, and 365 contractions, including nonrepresentable examples; and a padding checker verifies 86 distinguished pairs, with 16 initially nonspanning old sets and six distinguished-loop occurrences. Frozen proof-stage approvals, full replay instructions, and all-page PDF inspection records are included. These computations corroborate the ordinary argument and are not proof-assistant formalization or a literature-wide priority claim.
\mnote{The proof-stage approvals and visual-inspection records are shipped as
\path{79-two-element-audits-*.md}, \path{data/79-two-element-audits-*.json} and
\path{data/79-two-element-qa-visual-qa.json}; the PDF they inspect is not
shipped.}

%% ======================== source 80 ========================
\section{Three-tail obstructions and an all-rank first layer (source~80)}\label{mrn:src:80}
\paragraph{Abstract of source~80, as delivered.} We independently reconstruct the weighted rank-five threshold and higher-rank counterexamples already recorded in a ProveIt intake manuscript. A complete three-block graph gives an explicit last-Newton-gap violation at actual degree six, and the same argument supplies every higher degree, already for vertex-weighted height-two orders. Additional sections give a smaller graph obstruction to two higher-dimensional Lorentzian extensions whose own scalar sequences remain ultra-log-concave. Positive refinements include an all-rank first-layer Hessian inequality and full selected-tail cubic Lorentzianity for a three-vertex left shore. Exact solver-free checks accompany the examples, and the unchanged ordinary rank-five proof is bundled as a dependency.
\mnote{Source~80 is batch-73 manuscript~01,
\path{sharp-weighted-rank-boundary-result.zip} (title page ``Research note''),
pinned at commit \texttt{1512ef835}, the arrival of batch~72. ``The inspected
intake'' \cite{b:m74} is source~74's raw archive; \cite{b:m10} is source~10
(Section~\ref{mrn:src:10}) and \cite{b:m05} source~05
(Section~\ref{mrn:src:05}); the comparison is shipped as
\path{80-three-tail-INTAKE_COMPARISON.md}. Source~80 never cites \prp{}:
its Sections~\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:threshold}--\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:scalar} re-prove
source~74's rank threshold and Part~XI's rank-six family and are printed as
statements with pointers. New are Proposition~\ref{mrn:v:tt:prop:abstract},
the thirteen-vertex graph, the obstruction of
Section~\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:identify} and Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD};
Corollary~\ref{mrn:v:tt:cor:cubic} is source~10's theorem with a new proof.}

\paragraph{Relation to the inspected intake.}
The sharp weighted threshold, the complete-block quota construction and counterexamples in every rank at least six are explicitly present in the user-repository research manuscript \cite{b:m74}, at the pinned commit identified in the bibliography. Sections~\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:threshold}--\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:scalar} are an independent reconstruction and additional exact checking of those results, not a claim that they are new to that intake. Its \texttt{full\_lift.tex} also proves the two-tail graph theorem by a rank-compression lift. The three-vertex cubic statement is also in \cite{b:m10}; here an ordinary all-rank matrix argument replaces its finite determinant certificate. The source comparison is included; no external publication or global priority is claimed.

\subsection{The sharp weighted rank threshold}\label{mrn:v:tt:sec:threshold}
A bipartite endpoint support is a pair $(S,T)$ of equally sized shore subsets admitting a perfect matching. Count each feasible pair once, regardless of its matching witnesses. With nonnegative vertex activities, let $\gamma_k$ be the total product weight of size-$k$ supports and put $\Gamma(t)=\sum_k\gamma_kt^k$. Its surviving degree $d$ is the matching number of the positive-activity graph. Actual-degree ultra-log-concavity (ULC) means that $\gamma_k/\binom dk$ is log-concave; equivalently, with no internal zeros,
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:ULC}
 k(d-k)\gamma_k^2\ge(k+1)(d-k+1)\gamma_{k-1}\gamma_{k+1}
 \qquad(1\le k<d).
\end{equation}
\begin{theorem}[Weighted threshold from the inspected intake]\label{mrn:v:tt:thm:sharp}
Every independently nonnegative vertex-weighted finite bipartite endpoint-support polynomial of surviving matching rank at most five is actual-degree ULC. For every integer $d\ge6$, there is a finite bipartite graph with positive vertex activities and actual matching rank $d$ that violates the last inequality in \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:ULC}. The counterexamples may be oriented as vertex-weighted height-two strict orders.
\end{theorem}
The threshold is stated in \cite[Corollary 1.2 and \texttt{negative\_ranks.tex}]{b:m74}. The positive assertion is also proved independently in the ordinary result \cite[Corollary 5.2]{b:m79}; its unchanged six-page proof and frozen replayable archive are included as a dependency. The remaining assertions receive a self-contained proof below. Sharpness refers to rank, not the minimum number of vertices in a counterexample. No unweighted counterexample is asserted.
\mnote{This is source~74's Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive}, whose
rank-six witness is \prp's $H_{3,33}$ (Part~XI, Theorem~177.1); \cite{b:m79} is
source~79. The height-two orientation is \prp's native framework (its Part~II).
The proof below keeps source~80's displays and points to the derivations
already in this report and in \prp.}


\subsection{Complete-block scalar counterexamples}\label{mrn:v:tt:sec:scalar}
\subsubsection{The exact support formula}
Take disjoint shores $P\sqcup X$ and $Q\sqcup Y$, with sizes $p,m,q,n$, respectively. Include precisely all edges of $P\times Q$, $P\times Y$ and $X\times Q$. Give each core vertex in $P\cup Q$ activity $\lambda>0$, and every exterior vertex activity $1$.
\mnote{Source~80 derives the following count by Hall's condition: a support
with $i$ vertices of $P$ and $j$ of $Q$ is feasible exactly when $i+j\ge k$.
This is source~74's display~\eqref{mrn:w:rf:eq:Hallcount} with $a=p$, $b=q$,
core activity $w=\lambda$ (and source~77's
display~\eqref{mrn:v:rf4:eq:rank-five-coefficients}); for equal cores it is
\prp's Theorem~175.3 (Part~XI, \path{hb:thm:coefficients}). The derivation is
not reprinted.}

\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:quota}
 \gamma_k=\sum_{\substack{0\le i\le p,\ 0\le j\le q\\ i+j\ge k}}
 \binom pi\binom qj\lambda^{i+j}\binom m{k-i}\binom n{k-j},
\end{equation}
with out-of-range binomial coefficients interpreted as zero. This is an endpoint count, not a matching count.

If $m\ge q$ and $n\ge p$, the actual degree is $d=p+q$: the core is a cover of size $d$, and matching all of $P$ into $Y$ and all of $Q$ into $X$ supplies $d$ edges. Positive activities preserve this degree.

\subsubsection{An explicit degree-six violation}
\mnote{The family, the closed forms of $\gamma_4,\gamma_5,\gamma_6$ and the
factorization~\eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:factor} are \prp's Theorem~177.1 (Part~XI,
\path{hb:thm:rank6phase}) and its displays, with the same prefactor and
$T_N$ equal to Part~XI's $Q_n$; Part~XI proves more, the exact phase boundary.
Its $H_{3,33}$ with weight $26804$ is source~74's rank-six witness
(Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:negative}). The two numerical instances below are
new instances of that family and nothing more.}

Set $p=q=3$ and $m=n=N\ge3$, and abbreviate $A=\binom N2$, $B=\binom N3$. Formula \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:quota} gives
\begin{align*}
 \gamma_6&=\lambda^6B^2,\\
 \gamma_5&=\lambda^5(\lambda A^2+6AB),\\
 \gamma_4&=\lambda^4(\lambda^2N^2+6\lambda NA+9A^2+6NB).
\end{align*}
Consequently the last degree-six gap factors exactly as
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:factor}
 5\gamma_5^2-12\gamma_4\gamma_6
 =\frac{\lambda^{10}N^4(N-1)^2}{48}\,T_N(\lambda),
\end{equation}
where
\begin{align}
 T_N(\lambda)={}&(-N^2+34N-49)\lambda^2\nonumber\\
 &+12(N-1)(N-2)(N+3)\lambda\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:T}\\
 &+8(N-1)(N-2)^2(N+1).\nonumber
\end{align}
For integer $N\ge3$, the leading coefficient is negative exactly when $N\ge33$, since its positive root is $17+4\sqrt{15}$. Every such $N$ therefore gives a violation for sufficiently large finite $\lambda$.

For an independently checked round-number instance, choose $N=40$ and $\lambda=10000$. This is an 86-vertex graph with 249 edges, a six-vertex cover and a size-six matching. To shorten the certificate, put $g_k=\gamma_k/\lambda^k$, the coefficients of $\Gamma(t/\lambda)$. They are
\[
 (g_0,\ldots,g_6)=
 (1,90240,907219080,1024191381360,161879846800,6130238400,97614400).
\]
Their last gap is the negative integer
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:negative}
 5g_5^2-12g_4g_6=-1722535205514240000.
\end{equation}
A positive change of variable multiplies each gap by a positive factor, so this is an actual-degree ULC counterexample for the original activity assignment. Its earlier four gaps are positive. Within the same family, $N=33,\lambda=30000$ gives a 72-vertex example with scaled coefficients
\[
 (1,270198,8117832969,27178589394544,983239909344,8380804608,29767936)
\]
and last gap $-38842940605759488$. The intake already gives the same 72-vertex construction with the smaller activity $26804$ \cite{b:m74}. No minimum-vertex assertion is made.

\subsubsection{Failure at every higher rank}
\mnote{Source~80's argument here is source~74's
Section~\ref{mrn:w:rf:sec:negative}: the same limit of the top three
coefficients as the core activity grows, the same ratio
$pq/((p-1)(q-1))$, the same inequality
$(d+1)pq-2d(d-1)\ge d^2-4d-9>0$ for $d\ge6$, and $p=3$, $q=d-3$. It is not
reprinted.}


Finally orient all edges from the left shore to the right shore. No two nontrivial edges compose, so the relation is a height-two strict partial order. Adding reflexive pairs produces a preorder with the same disjoint-endpoint supports, since a loop cannot occur in a matching between disjoint endpoint sets. A single physical-vertex activity per vertex gives exactly the weights above; independent tail/head role activities are unnecessary. This completes the negative assertions of Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:sharp}.

\subsection{A separate multivariate obstruction and its graph lift}
For the abstract analogue, let $M$ have rank $q$ on $E\sqcup P$, where $P=\{A_0,A_1,A_2\}$ are distinguished elements. Let $Y$ be weighted heads with neighborhoods in $P$. A triple $(I,J,T)$, with $I\subseteq E$, $J\subseteq P$, $T\subseteq Y$, is feasible if some $K\subseteq J$ matches perfectly to $T$ and $I\cup(J\setminus K)$ is a basis of $M$. Define
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:transform}
 \mathcal F_M^{(3)}(\mathbf w,\mathbf a,z)
 =\sum_{(I,J,T)\text{ feasible}}w^I a^J
       \Bigl(\prod_{y\in T}v_y\Bigr)z^{3-|T|}.
\end{equation}
Each triple contributes once, even if its allocation $K$ is not unique. Its degree is $q+3$. Differentiation in an old nonloop variable gives the same construction for $M/e$, by restricting feasible triples to those containing $e$ and then deleting $e$.

We use the basic Lorentzian implications from Br\"and\'en--Huh \cite[Theorems 2.10 and 2.25]{b:BH}: nonnegative linear specialization and differentiation preserve Lorentzianity or zero, and a nonnegative quadratic Lorentzian polynomial has at most one positive Hessian eigenvalue. A binary quadratic with positive coefficients and negative discriminant therefore cannot be Lorentzian.


\subsubsection{Three parallel pairs}
Let $M$ have rank two on parallel pairs $\{u_i,A_i\}$, $i=0,1,2$, with every pair from distinct classes independent. It is real-representable: assign directions $(1,0),(0,1),(1,1)$ to the three classes. Give each $A_i$ one private head of activity $p$, and add one common head of activity $h$.
\begin{proposition}\label{mrn:v:tt:prop:abstract}
The transform \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:transform} is not Lorentzian whenever $h>0$ and $p>15h/4$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Selecting all three distinguished elements gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:quad}
 \partial_{a_0}\partial_{a_1}\partial_{a_2}\mathcal F_M^{(3)}
 =Rz^2+Bz(u_0+u_1+u_2)+C(u_0u_1+u_0u_2+u_1u_2),
\end{equation}
where
\[
 R=3p+h,\qquad B=2p^2+3ph,\qquad C=p^3+3p^2h.
\]
A single selected head leaves two distinguished basis elements, giving $R$. With one old element $u_i$, the two heads must match a distinguished pair containing $i$: two private pairs and all three private/common pairs qualify, giving $B$. With two old elements, either the three private heads or the common head with any two private heads qualify, giving $C$. These are endpoint counts, not allocation counts.

Set all $u_i=x$. The binary discriminant is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:disc}
 (3B)^2-4(3C)R=3p^2h(15h-4p)<0.
\end{equation}
Differentiation and nonnegative specialization yield the contradiction.
\end{proof}
At $p=4,h=1$, the quadratic is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:smallQ}
 Q=13z^2+44z(u_0+u_1+u_2)+112(u_0u_1+u_0u_2+u_1u_2).
\end{equation}
Its restriction $336x^2+132xz+13z^2$ has discriminant $-48$. Its Hessian on this two-dimensional subspace is $\left(\begin{smallmatrix}672&132\\132&26\end{smallmatrix}\right)$, positive definite with determinant $48$. The full four-dimensional Hessian has inertia $(2,2,0)$, since the two sum-zero old-variable directions have eigenvalue $-112$.

The seed itself is not transversal. Two parallel nonloops in any transversal presentation must share a singleton neighborhood; otherwise the pair can be matched. Three distinct nontrivial parallel classes would force three distinct singleton slots and hence an independent triple, contradicting rank two. This observation alone would not furnish a bipartite-graph counterexample. The following lift does.

\subsubsection{A thirteen-vertex graph with a full matching}\label{mrn:v:tt:sec:graph13}
Take disjoint shores
\[
 P\sqcup X=\{p_0,p_1,p_2\}\sqcup\{c,x_0,x_1\},\qquad
 Q\sqcup Y=\{q_0,q_1,q_2\}\sqcup\{y_0,y_1,y_2,h\}.
\]
The only edges are
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:edges}
 p_iq_i,\ p_iy_i,\ p_ih,\ cq_i\quad(0\le i\le2),
 \qquad x_0q_0,\ x_1q_1.
\end{equation}
There are fourteen edges. The set $P\cup Q$ is a $3+3$ cover. The matching
\[
 \{p_0y_0,p_1y_1,p_2y_2,x_0q_0,x_1q_1,cq_2\}
\]
has size six, which is maximal because there are six left vertices.

For individual selected-left variables, the natural degree-six support homogenization is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:graph}
 \mathcal F_G(\mathbf a,\mathbf z_Q,z)
 =\sum_{(S,T)\text{ feasible}}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in T}v_j\Bigr)a^S
         z^{3-|T\cap Y|}\prod_{j\in Q\setminus T}z_j.
\end{equation}
Give each $y_i$ activity $4$ and every other vertex activity $1$. Then
\[
 \left.\partial_{a_{p_0}}\partial_{a_{p_1}}\partial_{a_{p_2}}
      \partial_{a_c}\mathcal F_G\right|_{a_{x_0}=a_{x_1}=0}=Q,
\]
where the $u_i$ in \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:smallQ} are now the unused-head variables $z_i$. Thus \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:graph} is not Lorentzian, despite actual matching rank six.

Here is an exact matroid explanation of the equality. In the rank-three transversal matroid with slots $Q$, put both $A_i$ and an old dummy $u_i$ privately at slot $i$, and add the old universal element $c$. Contract $c$. A same-slot pair is dependent, any different-slot pair is independent, and the contraction has rank two; it is precisely the three-parallel-pair seed. Old-element contraction commutes with the Boolean allocation definition \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:transform}. Adding the two extra graph tails $x_0,x_1$ makes the full matching possible and setting their variables to zero leaves the displayed obstruction. Direct endpoint enumeration independently verifies the same equality.

The scalar support polynomial at these activities is
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:gamma1}
 \Gamma_1(t)=1+23t+195t^2+743t^3+1234t^4+744t^5+112t^6.
\end{equation}
It is strictly actual-degree ultra-log-concave; exact gaps are recorded below. Multivariate failure here is therefore not scalar failure.

\subsection{The failure survives identifying selected-tail variables}\label{mrn:v:tt:sec:identify}
Keep the graph \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:edges}. Give $p_0,p_1,p_2,c$ activity $L$, $x_0,x_1$ activity $1$, each private head $y_i$ activity $p$, and all other heads activity $1$. Replace every selected-left variable by its activity times $t$, and every unused $Q$ variable by $x$. Denote the resulting degree-six polynomial by $H(t,x,z)$.

Endpoint counting by the number of selected $Y$ heads gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:t4}
 [t^4]H=L^2(Ax^2+Bxz+Cz^2),
\end{equation}
with the local scalar coefficients
\begin{align*}
 A&=Lp^2(3L+2)(p+3),\\
 B&=p(6L^2p+9L^2+16Lp+32L+3p+6),\\
 C&=3L^2p+L^2+10Lp+8L+5p+5.
\end{align*}
For the positive integer choice $L=100,p=8$,
\[
 [t^4]H/10000=21260800x^2+4688240xz+258845z^2,
\]
and its discriminant is $-33412806400$. If $H$ were Lorentzian, the nonzero quadratic obtained from $\partial_t^4H$ at $t=0$ would be Lorentzian. Instead its Hessian is positive definite. This disproves even the three-variable $3+3$ cover-order Lorentzian analogue, with one selected-tail variable and full actual degree six.

The scalar coefficients in this second example are
\[
 (\gamma_0,\ldots,\gamma_6)=
 (1,3302,3846201,1806662900,262078850000,313024000000,70400000000).
\]
For either scalar sequence, actual-degree ultra-log-concavity means log-concavity of $\gamma_k/\binom6k$. Define the integer gap numerator
\[
 D_k=k(6-k)\gamma_k^2-(k+1)(7-k)\gamma_{k-1}\gamma_{k+1}.
\]
All five are positive in both examples:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}rrr@{}}\toprule
$k$&Activities of \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:gamma1}&$L=100,\ p=8$\\\midrule
1&305&8361608\\
2&47865&28862083622208\\
3&1118361&13248150548946090000\\
4&3890168&540999656224436000000000\\
5&1109184&268515910400000000000000\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Both sequences satisfy scalar ULC, despite failure of their chosen higher-dimensional homogenizations. The scalar counterexamples of Section~\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:scalar} are different graphs.
\mnote{These are obstructions of another kind than source~63's
Proposition~\ref{mrn:s:ne:prop:barrier} (Part~III), a twenty-vertex graph whose
full right marginal with unit left activities is not Lorentzian. Here the
thirteen-vertex graph's selected-tail homogenization and its three-variable
specialization fail, so Part~I's two-tail lifts (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:main},
Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:te:thm:main}) do not extend to three tails; the scalar
sequences stay $\ULC_6$, as Part~III expects.}


\subsection{An ordinary positive first-layer result for every rank}
Fix $r\ge2$ left vertices and an arbitrary finite set of heads with nonnegative activities $v_y$. Let $R_i$ be the total neighbor activity of tail $i$. For $i\ne j$, let $H_{ij}$ be the total common-neighbor activity, $Q_{ij}$ the sum of its squares, and $R_{ij}$ the sum of product activities over distinct head pairs matchable to $\{i,j\}$, counted once. Repeated-head choices and duplicate witnesses for common-head pairs give exactly
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:pair}
 R_{ij}=R_iR_j-\tfrac12(H_{ij}^2+Q_{ij}).
\end{equation}
The pair-count identity also underlies the neighbor-feature Gram argument in \cite[Section 2]{b:m05}. The following probability/projector proof gives a general-$r$ matrix statement by a different decomposition.
\begin{theorem}\label{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD}
The symmetric $r\times r$ matrix
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:N}
 N_{ii}=(r-1)R_i^2,\qquad
 N_{ij}=(r-1)R_iR_j-rR_{ij}\quad(i\ne j)
\end{equation}
is positive semidefinite for every such graph and activities.
\end{theorem}
\mnote{At $r=3$ the matrix $N$ is $3Q$ for source~10's matrix~$Q$
(display~\eqref{mrn:s:tv:eq:Q}, with $c_I=R_I$), so the case $r=3$ is
$Q\succeq0$, which source~10 proves by the 5339-monomial certificate of
Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:tv:lem:certificate}. For $r\ge4$ the theorem is new to the
repository. The identity~\eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:pair} is source~10's
display~\eqref{mrn:s:tv:eq:overlap}; the Gram argument cited from source~05 is
its display~\eqref{mrn:s:sn:eq:Gram}.}

\begin{proof}
First suppose all $R_i>0$. Define a probability distribution $p_i(y)=v_y/R_i$ on the neighbors of $i$ and zero elsewhere, and put $u_i=(\sqrt{p_i(y)})_y$. Set
\[
 A_{ij}=(u_i\cdot u_j)^2,\qquad
 C_{ii}=1,\quad C_{ij}=\sum_y p_i(y)p_j(y)\quad(i\ne j).
\]
Write $J=\mathbf1\mathbf1^T$. We claim that both $A$ and $C$ dominate $J/r$ in the positive-semidefinite order.

For any real vector $\xi$, the symmetric operator $T=\sum_i\xi_i u_i u_i^T$ acts on a space of dimension at most $r$, has trace $\sum_i\xi_i$, and has squared Hilbert--Schmidt norm $\xi^TA\xi$. Cauchy--Schwarz on its eigenvalues yields
\[
 \xi^TA\xi\ge\frac1r\Bigl(\sum_i\xi_i\Bigr)^2.
\]
Independently sample one label from each $p_i$. The equality partition $\pi$ of the $r$ sampled labels has at most $r$ blocks. Its matrix $K_\pi$ satisfies
\[
 \xi^TK_\pi\xi=\sum_{B\in\pi}\Bigl(\sum_{i\in B}\xi_i\Bigr)^2
       \ge\frac1r\Bigl(\sum_i\xi_i\Bigr)^2.
\]
Since $\mathbb E K_\pi=C$, the second claim follows. Both arguments allow arbitrary signed test vectors.

For $D=\diag(R_i)$, equation \eqref{mrn:v:tt:eq:pair} now gives
\begin{equation}\label{mrn:v:tt:eq:gram}
 D^{-1}ND^{-1}=\frac r2(A+C)-J
 =\frac r2(A-J/r)+\frac r2(C-J/r)\succeq0.
\end{equation}
If $R_i=0$, the corresponding row and column of $N$ vanish. Apply the proof to the remaining indices with the original denominator $r$: their span dimension and number of partition blocks are still at most $r$. This includes all zero-activity boundaries.
\end{proof}

For a left subset $S$, let $R_S$ sum the weights of the head subsets matchable to $S$, counted once. Define the selected-tail homogeneous polynomial
\[
 F(z,\mathbf a)=\sum_{S\subseteq[r]}R_Sa^S z^{r-|S|},\qquad R_\varnothing=1.
\]
Then
\[
 \partial_z^{r-2}F=\frac{r!}{2}z^2+(r-1)!z\sum_iR_ia_i
                +(r-2)!\sum_{i<j}R_{ij}a_ia_j.
\]
After division by $(r-2)!$, its Hessian has upper-left entry $r(r-1)>0$ and Schur complement
\[
 B-\frac{r-1}{r}RR^T=-N/r,\qquad B_{ii}=0,\quad B_{ij}=R_{ij}.
\]
Thus this first-layer Hessian has exactly one positive eigenvalue. This ordinary all-rank statement is compatible with the higher-layer obstructions above and does not assert full Lorentzianity for arbitrary $r$.


\subsubsection{The full three-tail rank-zero base}
The following statement appears in \cite[Theorem 1.1]{b:m10}, whose proof uses a 5,339-term determinant certificate. Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD} gives an ordinary replacement for that certificate.
\begin{corollary}\label{mrn:v:tt:cor:cubic}
For a bipartite graph whose entire left shore has three vertices, with arbitrary nonnegative head activities, the polynomial
\[
 F=z^3+z^2\sum_iR_ia_i+z\sum_{i<j}R_{ij}a_ia_j+R_{012}a_0a_1a_2
\]
is Lorentzian, retaining each selected-tail variable individually.
\end{corollary}
\mnote{This is source~10's Theorem~\ref{mrn:s:tv:thm:marginal} (Part~III),
with $s,z_i,c_I$ there written $z,a_i,R_I$ here, and the proof below is a
second route to it: its Rayleigh step is source~10's
display~\eqref{mrn:s:tv:eq:rayleigh}, and Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD}
replaces the certificate Lemma~\ref{mrn:s:tv:lem:certificate} as the route to
$Q\succeq0$. The certificate itself stays a valid proof.}

\begin{proof}
After deleting zero-weight heads, matchable left subsets are the independent sets of a transversal matroid $L$. Take its direct sum with three free dummy elements and truncate to rank three. Identify the dummy variables with $z$ in the basis polynomial. The resulting Lorentzian polynomial has exactly the same exponent support as $F$, so this support is $M$-convex. Only support equality is used; the coefficients need not agree. This also handles a rank drop or an empty positive graph.

Next use a rank-three transversal matroid on the heads and three private dummies $d_i$, with the left vertices as slots. Its basis polynomial, after substituting head activities, is
\[
 B(\mathbf d)=d_0d_1d_2+\sum_iR_i\prod_{j\ne i}d_j
                   +\sum_{i<j}R_{ij}d_k+R_{012},
\]
where $k$ is the remaining index. Wagner's theorem that every rank-three matroid is Rayleigh \cite[Theorem 1.1]{b:Wagner}, applied to $d_j,d_k$ after $d_i=0$, yields
\[
 R_{ij}R_{ik}-R_iR_{012}\ge0.
\]
Zero activities are included by continuity. The Hessian of $\partial_{a_i}F$ in $(z,a_j,a_k)$ is
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}2R_i&R_{ij}&R_{ik}\\R_{ij}&0&R_{012}\\R_{ik}&R_{012}&0\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad \det=2R_{012}(R_{ij}R_{ik}-R_iR_{012})\ge0.
\]
If $R_{012}>0$, its bottom block has one positive and one negative eigenvalue, so the determinant excludes a second positive eigenvalue. If $R_{012}=0$, the arrow matrix with zero bottom block directly has at most one positive eigenvalue. Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD} supplies the remaining derivative $\partial_zF$. The support condition and all four quadratic-derivative tests prove Lorentzianity \cite[Theorem 2.25]{b:BH}.
\end{proof}
This is the zero-old-rank base of the three-tail construction, not a positive-rank extension. It is therefore consistent with the positive-old-rank multivariate counterexamples above.

\subsection{Scope and exact reproducibility}
The reconstructed Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:sharp}, already present in \cite{b:m74}, gives the exact universal \emph{weighted} matching-rank threshold: actual-degree ULC holds through five and can fail at every rank six and above. The explicit complete-block examples use positive integer vertex activities and also yield height-two order counterexamples. They establish no unweighted counterexample and no minimum-vertex bound.

The smaller thirteen-vertex examples serve a different purpose. Their scalar sequences are ULC; their saturated six-vertex left shore already puts them within the ordinary one-shore transversal-basis guarantee. They instead disprove the specified multivariate and three-variable Lorentzian extensions. Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD} proves a first-layer condition at every rank, and Corollary~\ref{mrn:v:tt:cor:cubic} proves full Lorentzianity at the three-tail zero-old-rank base. No physical tail/head collision merge is used.

The package includes exact standard-library checkers for the new scalar and multivariate examples and the positive matrix statements, together with the unchanged rank-five proof archive and readable PDF. The scalar checker independently checks all 78 quota Hall cases, reconstructs the coefficients and verifies the exact last-gap factorization. The direct-union appendix is corroborated by 1,672 identities over 337,428 candidate bases, including 352 zero-parameter interpolation checks. The earlier graph recounts find 397 supports among 1,716 candidates; the abstract union checker finds 109 bases among 210 candidates. Matrix corroboration covers 2,800 weighted cases with 8,400 exact PSD tests, plus 6,602 three-tail cases with 46,214 principal-minor checks. The ordinary results do not depend on finite numerical sampling.

Approved proof notes, source-bound mathematical reviews, deterministic receipts and all-page PDF inspections are included. Replay uses temporary copies, compares every deterministic field, rejects Python optimization mode and verifies release immutability, including the frozen rank-five dependency. The new checkers need only the Python standard library; the older dependency uses SymPy. The source comparison credits the overlapping intake constructions; the incoming source scripts are not executed. No proof-assistant formalization, literature-wide priority claim or external publication is asserted.

\paragraph{Appendix~A of source~80.}
\mnote{Source~80's appendix, \emph{A direct all-matroid extension of the intake
lift}, is printed in Section~\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:unionappendix} as the first
proof of source~79's Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:te:thm:main}.}

%% ======================== questions of Part V ========================
\section{Questions raised by Part~V}\label{mrn:v:sec:questions}
The following are open after Part~V. They are written in this merge: the
first is suggested by source~80's obstructions, the others are the sources'
own closing questions.
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{A three-tail Lorentzian homogenization} (source~80). For a cover
  with three vertices on one shore, the selected-tail homogenization and its
  three-variable specialization fail on a thirteen-vertex graph
  (Sections~\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:graph13} and~\ref{mrn:v:tt:sec:identify}), and
  source~63's right marginal fails on a twenty-vertex graph
  (Proposition~\ref{mrn:s:ne:prop:barrier}), while the first layer
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD}) and the zero-old-rank cubic
  (Corollary~\ref{mrn:v:tt:cor:cubic}) hold. With arbitrary activities on
  both shores no Lorentzian homogenization of order $\nu(G)$ can exist for
  all $3+q$ covers, since it would make \prp's $H_{3,33}$ $\ULC_6$. Under
  which restrictions (unit or one-shore activities, restricted cores) does
  one exist, and which avoids these obstructions?
\item \emph{The two-tail/three-head role cover} (source~78). For directed
  endpoint supports with a role cover of two tails and three heads, does rank
  normalization hold? It concerns the directed setting of \prp's Part~XII and
  is not addressed by Parts~I--V.
\item \emph{Larger aggregate realizations} (source~77). Which larger cover
  patterns admit aggregate Hermitian realizations of $p_G$, and can the
  rank-four constructions give quantitative deficit bounds? Equality is
  already classified by source~72's Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:se:thm:main}.
\end{itemize}

02 & \path{ProveIt_Four_Plus_Four_Top_Gap.zip}: \emph{The Top Conditional Gap for Four Plus Four Covers} (6 pages) & none & \texttt{mrn:e:ff:} & Section~\ref{mrn:src:02}\\
01 & \path{ProveIt_Eventual_One_Shore_Scaling_Through_Rank_Eight.zip}: \emph{Eventual One Shore Scaling Through Rank Eight} (8 pages) & none & \texttt{mrn:e:r8:} & Section~\ref{mrn:src:01}\\
\end{longtable}

The full commit hashes are \path{1b3960d8acdabd983147b7c609bd9dfb2d67a0a0}
(the batch-70 archive of the manuscript now printed as Part~XII of \prp),
\texttt{a866ff9a2} (the batch-68 archive of the Hall-bottleneck manuscript,
now Part~XI) and \path{4a3b6991d0cc12cc09b884e22728e2128a54c6e3} (the commit
that wrote Part~XI). Source~72 cites source~74 by title, and its shipped
\path{72-strictness-SOURCES.md} records a hash of source~74's delivered PDF;
that file is kept as delivered.

\paragraph{Batch~73 (Part~V).}
Four further sources arrived in commit \texttt{f8c3a392a} (batch~73, cluster~C1
of fifteen archives) and were placed in commit \texttt{5e4f1263c}. They are
numbered 77--80 here, continuing the batch-72 numbers in order of dependency;
their batch-73 manuscript numbers are 26, 08, 02 and~01, and number~78 here has
nothing to do with batch~72's retired archive~78. Each is dated 1 October 2026.
Their PDFs, manuscripts, delivery READMEs and checksum ledgers are not shipped;
their code, data, audit and provenance files are, under the prefixes
\texttt{77-rank-four-}, \texttt{78-two-three-star-}, \texttt{79-two-element-}
and \texttt{80-three-tail-} (README of this report). The report is now built
from 31 sources.

78 & 08, \path{all-two-by-three-cores-result.zip}: \emph{Weighted matching supports with a two plus three core} (6 pages, A4) & none & \texttt{mrn:v:st:} & Section~\ref{mrn:src:78}\\

The full hashes are \path{d5e863bba6b40738af292b45005a0cf0495903e4} (the
batch-70 write of \prp's Part~XII; source~77 also records the article blob
\path{2b952dfcf443cdb014878c4bc0cd6adc1f8ef7e5} and the line numbers it read,
all exact there) and \path{1512ef8356d10fef99e7caaa2753620f0c900c83} (the
arrival of batch~72). Sources~78 and~79 cite only their batch-73 siblings, by
archive name. Source~77's shipped \path{77-rank-four-SOURCE.txt} also records a
SHA-256 for the inspected article that the pinned blob does not reproduce; the
README of this report gives the details. Source~80's shipped
\path{80-three-tail-INTAKE_COMPARISON.md} compares it with the raw batch-72
archives of sources~74 and~10 at \texttt{1512ef835}; it does not compare with
this report or with \prp, and so misses that source~80's scalar family is
Part~XI's.

\emph{Batch-73 archives that are not sources.} Five further archives of the
cluster concern the same object and are duplicates; they are not staged and
survive only in commit \texttt{f8c3a392a}:
\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{matching-rank-four-package~(1).zip} (manuscript~27) is the first,
  twelve-page edition of source~77; every result of it is in source~77.
\item \path{complete-two-by-three-result.zip} (manuscript~09), \emph{Independent
  weights on a complete two plus three matching core}, proves source~75's
  Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:fh:thm:classification} at $s=3$ (its graph is
  $H_{2,3;n,m}$) and is the core mask~63 of source~78, which supersedes it;
  source~78 cites its last-gap computation and nonreal example.
\item \path{weighted-bipartite-rank-five-result.zip} (manuscript~03),
  \emph{Weighted bipartite matching supports through rank five}, proves
  source~74's Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:main} and
  Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive} by a third route, an explicit positive
  semidefinite decomposition; source~79 generalizes its construction.
\item \path{two-pair-exterior-result.zip} (manuscript~05), \emph{Weighted support
  inequalities for overlapping pair neighborhoods}, proves a case of source~74's
  theorem (a $2+3$ cover), without the strictness that source~72 gives.
\item \path{twin-core-lorentzian-result.zip} (manuscript~04), \emph{Lorentzian
  polynomials for twin core matching supports}, proves cases of source~74's
  theorem; its private-head theorem is \prp's leaf compression with its ambient
  theorem (Lemma~206.1, Theorem~70.1).
\end{itemize}
A sixth retired archive of the cluster, \texttt{common-path-rank-package~(1).zip},
belongs to the report \path{constrained-crossover-closure}, which also receives
the cluster's two crossover-rank manuscripts. Three archives of the cluster on
directed endpoint supports (\path{physical-cover-three-result.zip},
\path{three-core-rayleigh-result.zip},
\path{bipartite-and-pendant-support-result.zip}) are placed with the batch's
directed-support manuscripts, not here.

\subsection{What each source contributed}\label{mrn:sec:contrib}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{74} (base): the $2+q$ transversal lift
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:rf:thm:main}), weighted normalization through matching
  number five and the least weighted failure rank six
  (Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:rankfive}), the cover obstruction
  (Corollary~\ref{mrn:w:rf:cor:coverobstruction}), the forced-sector and
  scaling refinements, the negative family at every rank $\ge6$, the Ehrhart
  corollary, the generic-line matroid model, the sharp all-core constants
  $2q/(q-1)$ and three, the star-core transfer proofs, a second route to the
  first-gap equality case, and quantitative strictness thresholds.
\item \textbf{72}: equality in any one gap only for balanced star forests,
  and strict $\ULC_r$ otherwise, under the cover hypothesis of source~74
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:se:thm:main}); a weighted Chan--Pak contraction
  criterion (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:se:thm:weighted}); a second route to the
  first-gap equality case.
\item \textbf{76}: real stability of the basis polynomial, hence
  real-rootedness of $p_G$, for every graph with a vertex cover of two
  vertices on each shore (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:sb:thm:main}); a second route to
  weighted rank four; the sharp stability criterion for complete $2\times s$
  mixed cores (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:sb:thm:two-s-boundary}) and its 17-vertex
  boundary example.
\item \textbf{75}: the full Hall classification
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:w:fh:thm:classification}), with a second route to its
  positive half, strictness, real-rootedness for a singleton core, explicit
  asymmetric failures, and a strictly ultra-log-concave rank-five example
  with nonreal zeros.
\item \textbf{71}: a connected all-unit graph with 6916 vertices and matching
  number 3450 that is not $\ULC_{3450}$ (Theorem~\ref{mrn:u:uw:thm:main}); a
  deficiency-one example and infinitely many such; failure of the two
  Davis--Kohl $h^*$-polynomials.
\item \textbf{73}: one-shore counterexamples of matching number 50 (125
  vertices) and 38; the endpoint quadratic and a construction criterion; the
  weighted pendant-arm identity (which contains source~71's) and a rank-310
  transfer example.
\item \textbf{55}: for one common right-core activity on full Hall graphs, the
  least matching number with a failing last gap is exactly 38, with the two
  shapes and minimal populations (864 vertices).
\item \textbf{47}: in the same family no intermediate gap fails through
  matching number 38, so 38 is the least failure rank of full $\ULC$.
\item \textbf{51}: a rank-49 full Hall graph whose last gap holds for every
  activity while the penultimate gap fails.
\item \textbf{60}: Lorentzian lift for matrices with a rank-two exterior
  column block; exact Hall-slack conditioning (slack zero, one, two); forcing
  $a-2$ exterior-right vertices of a genuine $a+q$ cover leaves a
  $\ULC_{q+2}$ factor; eventual strictness with an explicit threshold.
\item \textbf{10}: the degree-three left marginal of any graph with three left
  vertices is Lorentzian (a 5339-monomial determinant certificate); star cores
  on the three-vertex shore give $\ULC_{q+3}$ for all activities on both
  shores, hence all five rank-six inequalities for both star orientations.
\item \textbf{23}: rooted sector marginals are Lorentzian; the 25-vertex graph
  whose all-$B$ conditional cubic fails both unshifted Newton inequalities
  although the graph is strictly $\ULC_6$ for every common core activity.
\item \textbf{13}: the last rank-six inequality for every one-shore graph and
  every finite activity (858 weighted squares, ten profiles).
\item \textbf{05}: the second rank-six inequality likewise, through a
  Lorentzian cubic truncation (a certificate of 50,037,694 coefficients); the
  quintic route is refuted.
\item \textbf{69}: $\ULC_6$ for every $3\times3$ core with complete exterior
  neighbourhoods (8,964 certificate vectors); a private-leaf reduction; a
  barrier coefficient $56-4n$.
\item \textbf{63}: $\ULC_6$ for the complete core with nested exterior
  neighbourhoods; a 20-vertex graph whose full right marginal is not
  Lorentzian; forcing four right vertices gives $c_1^2\ge3c_0c_2$.
\item \textbf{08s}: the top conditional gap for every $a+b$ cover with
  constant $a(b+2)^2/((a-1)(b^2+b+2))$; the two-plane incidence lemma in rank
  $b$; eventual strict $\ULC$ for $3+3$, $3+4$, $3+5$ covers; the reduction of
  rank seven to the $4+3$ middle gap; a weighted local-equality lemma; the
  bracket $[7,38]$ (superseded).
\item \textbf{21}: for every $3+3$ core, $(n-1)C_1^2\ge2nC_0C_2$ (also with
  arbitrary left activities) and $C_2^2\ge\frac{21}{8}C_1C_3$ (refined to
  $75/28$); eventual strict $\ULC_6$; the global nonstrict rank-six statement.
\item \textbf{43, 38, 35, 32, 29}: second routes to source~21's theorem for
  complete, matching-hole, two-complete-row, two-complete-column and
  $\ge6$-edge cores, with the partition formula and elementary proof of~43,
  the orbit census of~29, the every-field corollaries of~38, 35, 32, 29, and
  their certificates.
\item \textbf{08}: the $4+3$ middle gap $C_2^2\ge\frac{15}{8}C_1C_3$; eventual
  $\ULC_\nu$ through matching number seven; $[8,38]$.
\item \textbf{03}: eventual strict $\ULC_8$ for every $5+3$ cover (factors
  $77/48$, $7/4$, $55/24$).
\item \textbf{02}: the $4+4$ top gap $16/7$ with a coupled correction; the
  unrestricted upper incidence bound is false (six real vectors).
\item \textbf{01}: the $4+4$ middle gap; eventual $\ULC_\nu$ through matching
  number eight; the persistent-failure bracket $[9,38]$.
\item \textbf{77} (batch~73): at unit activities and matching number three, a
  nonreal zero needs fifteen vertices, with a unique extremal graph
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:rf4:thm:minimum}, computer-assisted), the
  pair-neighbourhood family $p_n$ and its discriminant, and the twin remark;
  second routes to source~76's $2+2$ real-rootedness (univariate), to \prp's
  $1+2$ real-rootedness, and to weighted rank four; a certificate for one
  block-constant rank-five family.
\item \textbf{78} (batch~73): a computer-assisted second route for $2\times3$
  cores with complete exteriors (26 exact certificates); real stability for
  empty and star cores, new at the exceptional two-neighbour seed.
\item \textbf{79} (batch~73): the two-element Lorentzian deformation of every
  matroid basis polynomial (Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:te:thm:main}), by a
  principal-line seed and contraction; second routes to source~74's graph
  theorem with every left variable kept and to weighted rank five.
\item \textbf{80} (batch~73): the all-rank first-layer theorem
  (Theorem~\ref{mrn:v:tt:thm:PSD}) and with it a certificate-free proof of
  source~10's three-vertex theorem; an abstract three-element obstruction and
  two thirteen-vertex graph obstructions to three-tail Lorentzian lifts; the
  union proof of source~79's theorem; two further numerical instances of
  \prp's rank-six family.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Where the merge chose}\label{mrn:sec:choices}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Placement and base.} The batch's manuscripts continue Parts~XI--XIII
  of \prp{} and answer several of its research questions, but they form a
  self-contained programme of 26 archives; they were placed as a new report in
  \path{log-concavity-and-unimodality}, with source~74 as the base because it
  proves the most general theorem and the others cite it. \prp{} is cited,
  not reprinted, and receives dated pointers to this report.
\item \emph{Source~74's layout.} Its eight section files were inlined into
  \texttt{article.tex} and removed; its appendices are ordinary sections of
  Part~I. Its two short lemmas that restate \prp{} (Lemmas~\ref{mrn:w:rf:lem:small}
  and~\ref{mrn:w:rf:lem:singleton}) keep their statements and receive pointer
  proofs. Table~\ref{mrn:w:tab:shapes} was added from its recorded verifier
  output.
\item \emph{Re-proofs of \prp.} Sources~74 and~72 re-prove Part~XI's
  first-gap inequality and its equality case by the Motzkin--Straus route:
  both proofs are printed, marked as second routes. Source~74's rank-six
  witness is Part~XI's $H_{3,33}$ and its $(4,4)$ shape Part~XI's $H_{4,11}$:
  printed with notes. The leaf compression of sources~72, 74 and~76 is
  Part~XIII's; source~76's inversion identity, gluing lemma,
  expected-determinant lemma and $K_{2,k}$ stability are Part~VI's: proofs
  of those are pointers.
\item \emph{Same theorem in the batch.} Source~76's weighted rank four and
  the positive half of source~75's classification are cases of source~74's
  theorem; both are printed in full as second routes, because their proofs
  (real stability; a comparison polynomial) differ from source~74's and give
  more (real-rootedness, strictness).
\item \emph{Stale sentences.} Statements of sources~75 and~76 that the first
  weighted failure rank is five or six, or that rank five is open, are kept
  with notes: source~74 settles them.
\item \emph{Part~II.} Source~71's pendant lemma is printed once, in source~73's
  weighted form (source~71's statement is kept with a pointer proof).
  Source~47's first-gap proof is a pointer to Part~XI of \prp{} (its
  strictness step is kept); its recall of source~55's endpoint theorem is kept
  with pointers to source~55, which is printed before it. Source~73's
  rank-310 example is kept as a remark; it is one-shore, not unit, weighted.
  Statements of sources~55, 47 and~51 that call rank-six one-shore $\ULC$
  open or that leave earlier gaps open are kept with notes.
\item \emph{Part~III.} Source~60's restatement of Part~XII's rank-three
  matrix is a pointer. The 25-vertex graph is printed once, in source~23;
  source~05's description of it is kept with a note. Source~13's incidence
  lemma, which is also source~21's (Part~IV) and a case of source~08s's, is
  printed with source~13's proof, where it is first used. The first-gap
  arguments of sources~69 and~63 are pointers to \prp's Theorem~180.2.
  Source~05's direct proof of its rank-two lemma is kept as a second route of
  source~60's theorem. Sentences of sources~10, 13, 23, 60, 63 and 69 that
  call three middle gaps, or the whole rank-six one-shore problem, open are
  kept with notes giving the status above.
\item \emph{Part~IV.} Source~08s, delivered only inside archive~08, is
  printed as a source of its own and is the base of Part~IV, because it
  proves the general $a+b$ theorem of which source~21's is the $3+3$ case.
  Source~21 is printed in full as that case; its incidence-lemma proof is a
  pointer to source~13's printed proof. The five special-core sources are
  printed in full as second routes of source~21's theorem (the union rule;
  the alternative of shipping only their provenance was not taken). Source~08s's
  appendix proof of its weighted equality lemma is a pointer to source~72's
  proof, and its one equation label \texttt{eq:kernel} is kept on the
  displayed decomposition. The brackets $[7,38]$ and $[8,38]$ are kept as
  history with notes; $[9,38]$ is final. Source~08s's claim that
  Choe--Wagner's printed counts are wrong is labelled as its own statement.
\item \emph{Part~V (batch~73).} The four kept batch-73 manuscripts were added as
  one Part after Part~IV, not inserted into Parts~I and~III: Parts~I--IV were
  written the same day, and \prp's reciprocal notes cite their section and
  theorem numbers, which Part~V leaves unchanged. Parts~I and~III receive five
  dated forward notes instead. Four Parts of a page or two of new mathematics
  each were not made. The order (77, 78, 79, 80) is that of dependency.
\item \emph{Re-proofs in Part~V.} Statements that repeat Parts~I--IV or \prp{}
  are kept with notes. Proofs are replaced by pointers where they are the
  repository's proofs (source~77's ambient theorem, leaf compression and thin
  covers; source~80's derivation of the quota count and its higher-rank limit
  argument), and printed as marked second routes where they differ: source~77's
  path and Gram-determinant proof of $2+2$ real-rootedness, its rooted-path proof
  of the $1+2$ lemma (used by the rank-three minimum) and its rank-five
  certificate; source~78's 26 certificates; source~79's proofs of its graph
  corollaries; source~80's proof of the three-tail cubic. Source~80's scalar
  counterexample displays and its two numerical instances are kept, with the
  attribution to Part~XI of \prp{} that source~80 omits.
\item \emph{One theorem, two proofs.} Source~79's theorem is printed once, in
  source~79's section; source~80's one-page matroid-union proof (its
  Appendix~A, which credits source~74's lift) is printed first, as the shorter
  proof, and source~79's own proof second.
\item \emph{Source~80's three-tail cubic.} It is printed in Part~V as a second
  route to source~10's theorem, with a dated note in source~10's section, not
  inside that section.
\item \emph{Stale sentences in Part~V.} Source~77's closing statement that the
  least weighted failure rank is five or six, the same sentence in its shipped
  \path{77-rank-four-REVISION.md}, source~77's claim to answer three research
  questions of \prp, and source~78's delivered README on rank five are kept
  with notes giving the current status.
\end{itemize}
